Notice that not all the polynomials have changed, however we preferred to keep the notation uniform for the new basis. Therefore, in what follows, our efforts will focus on constructing the polynomial algebra generated by the following set composed of twenty polynomials up to degree nine (five central elements and fifteen generators):

(3.43)

\mathcal{P}:=\{\bar{b}_{1},\bar{b}_{2},\bar{b}_{3},\bar{c}_{1},\bar{C}_{2},\bar{d}_{1},\bar{D}_{2},\bar{D}_{3},\bar{D}_{4},\bar{E}_{1},\bar{F}_{1},\bar{F}_{2},\bar{F}_{3},\bar{F}_{4},\bar{G}_{1},\bar{G}_{2},\bar{H}_{1},\bar{H}_{2},\bar{I}_{1},\bar{I}_{2}\}\,.

Hence
\textbf{Alg}\left\langle\mathcal{P}\right\rangle
generates a polynomial algebra with
\dim_{FL}\textbf{Alg}\left\langle\mathcal{P}\right\rangle=20.
In the next section, we will show that
\textbf{Alg}\left\langle\mathcal{P}\right\rangle
is closed under the Poisson bracket
\{\cdot,\cdot\}.
Additionally, we should highlight that the two missing label operators, which Moshinsky and Nagel discussed in
[
19
]
and represented by two commuting
\mathfrak{su}(2)\times\mathfrak{su}(2)
scalars within the enveloping algebra of
\mathfrak{su}(4)
, can be expressed as follows:

(3.44)

\Omega=C^{(111)}=\bar{C}_{2}\,,\qquad\Phi=C^{(202)}+C^{(022)}-C^{(112)}=\bar{d}_{1}+2\bar{D}_{4}\,.

4.
The construction of the polynomial algebra

In this Section
4
, we analyze the structure of the polynomial algebra generated by the set
\mathcal{P}
in detail. In order to simplify the computations, we apply the grading method initially introduced in
[
24
]
to reduce the number of monomials predicted from the compact forms (i.e., just based on the degree). In order to understand the precise features of the polynomial algebra,
we inspect the formal expansions that arise as a consequence of the analysis of the degree associated with the Poisson brackets. For each degree, besides the Poisson bracket relations, there can also exist additional algebraic relations involving the above polynomials, i.e. relations of the type

(4.1)

\displaystyle\text{P}(\bar{\mathbf{B}},\bar{\mathbf{C}},\bar{\mathbf{D}},\bar{\mathbf{E}},\bar{\mathbf{F}},\bar{\mathbf{G}},\bar{\mathbf{H}},\bar{\mathbf{I}})=0\,,

where we have indicated formally polynomial expansions of a given degree (in the underlying Lie algebra generators) involving the generators in the set (
3.43
). Let us begin by observing that the five elements
\{\bar{b}_{1},\bar{b}_{2},\bar{b}_{3},\bar{c}_{1},\bar{d}_{1}\}
are central, and thus their Berezin brackets with any generator is zero. Again, the relations begin at degree six, where we can write the formal relation:

(4.2)

\{\bar{\mathbf{C}},\bar{\mathbf{D}}\}\sim\bar{\mathbf{F}}+\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{D}}+\bar{\mathbf{C}}\bar{\mathbf{C}}+\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{B}}\,.

At degree seven, for Berezin brackets involving
\{\bar{\mathbf{C}},\bar{\mathbf{E}}\}
and
\{\bar{\mathbf{D}},\bar{\mathbf{D}}\}
, we have a formal expansion of the type:

(4.3)

\{\bar{\mathbf{C}},\bar{\mathbf{E}}\}\sim\{\bar{\mathbf{D}},\bar{\mathbf{D}}\}\sim\bar{\mathbf{G}}+\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{E}}+\bar{\mathbf{C}}\bar{\mathbf{D}}+\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{C}}\,.

Degree eight expansions involve the Berezin brackets
\{\bar{\mathbf{C}},\bar{\mathbf{F}}\}
and
\{\bar{\mathbf{D}},\bar{\mathbf{E}}\}
through the following formal expansion:

(4.4)

\{\bar{\mathbf{C}},\bar{\mathbf{F}}\}\sim\{\bar{\mathbf{D}},\bar{\mathbf{E}}\}\sim\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{F}}+\bar{\mathbf{C}}\bar{\mathbf{E}}+\bar{\mathbf{D}}\bar{\mathbf{D}}+\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{C}}\bar{\mathbf{C}}+\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{D}}+\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{B}}\,.

Poisson brackets involving degree nine expansions are
\{\bar{\mathbf{C}},\bar{\mathbf{G}}\}
,
\{\bar{\mathbf{D}},\bar{\mathbf{F}}\}
, and involve the following formal relation:

(4.5)

\{\bar{\mathbf{C}},\bar{\mathbf{G}}\}\sim\{\bar{\mathbf{D}},\bar{\mathbf{F}}\}\sim\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{G}}+\bar{\mathbf{C}}\bar{\mathbf{F}}+\bar{\mathbf{D}}\bar{\mathbf{E}}+\bar{\mathbf{C}}\bar{\mathbf{C}}\bar{\mathbf{C}}+\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{C}}\bar{\mathbf{D}}+\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{E}}+\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{C}}\,.

For degree-ten relations, which involve the Berezin brackets
\{\bar{\mathbf{C}},\bar{\mathbf{H}}\}
,
\{\bar{\mathbf{D}},\bar{\mathbf{G}}\}
and
\{\bar{\mathbf{E}},\bar{\mathbf{F}}\}
, we have the following:

\displaystyle\{\bar{\mathbf{C}},\bar{\mathbf{H}}\}\sim\{\bar{\mathbf{D}},\bar{\mathbf{G}}\}\sim\{\bar{\mathbf{E}},\bar{\mathbf{F}}\}\ \sim\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{H}}

\displaystyle+\bar{\mathbf{C}}\bar{\mathbf{G}}+\bar{\mathbf{D}}\bar{\mathbf{F}}+\bar{\mathbf{E}}\bar{\mathbf{E}}+\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{F}}+\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{C}}\bar{\mathbf{E}}+\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{D}}\bar{\mathbf{D}}

(4.6)

\displaystyle+\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{C}}\bar{\mathbf{C}}+\bar{\mathbf{C}}\bar{\mathbf{C}}\bar{\mathbf{D}}+\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{D}}+\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{B}}\,.

Degree eleven Poisson brackets are given by
\{\bar{\mathbf{C}},\bar{\mathbf{I}}\}
,
\{\bar{\mathbf{D}},\bar{\mathbf{H}}\}
,
\{\bar{\mathbf{E}},\bar{\mathbf{G}}\}
and
\{\bar{\mathbf{F}},\bar{\mathbf{F}}\}
through the following expansion:

\displaystyle\{\bar{\mathbf{C}},\bar{\mathbf{I}}\}\sim\{\bar{\mathbf{D}},\bar{\mathbf{H}}\}\sim\{\bar{\mathbf{E}},\bar{\mathbf{G}}\}\sim\{\bar{\mathbf{F}},\bar{\mathbf{F}}\}\sim\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{I}}+\bar{\mathbf{C}}\bar{\mathbf{H}}

\displaystyle+\bar{\mathbf{D}}\bar{\mathbf{G}}+\bar{\mathbf{E}}\bar{\mathbf{F}}+\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{C}}\bar{\mathbf{F}}+\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{D}}\bar{\mathbf{E}}+\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{C}}\bar{\mathbf{C}}\bar{\mathbf{C}}+\bar{\mathbf{C}}\bar{\mathbf{D}}\bar{\mathbf{D}}

(4.7)

\displaystyle+\bar{\mathbf{C}}\bar{\mathbf{C}}\bar{\mathbf{E}}+\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{G}}+\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{C}}\bar{\mathbf{D}}+\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{E}}+\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{C}}\,.

Degree twelve expansions involve
\{\bar{\mathbf{D}},\bar{\mathbf{I}}\}
,
\{\bar{\mathbf{E}},\bar{\mathbf{H}}\}
,
\{\bar{\mathbf{F}},\bar{\mathbf{G}}\}
through the following formal expansion:

\displaystyle\{\bar{\mathbf{D}},\bar{\mathbf{I}}\}\sim\{\bar{\mathbf{E}},\bar{\mathbf{H}}\}\sim\{\bar{\mathbf{F}},\bar{\mathbf{G}}\}\sim\bar{\mathbf{C}}\bar{\mathbf{I}}

\displaystyle+\bar{\mathbf{D}}\bar{\mathbf{H}}+\bar{\mathbf{E}}\bar{\mathbf{G}}+\bar{\mathbf{F}}\bar{\mathbf{F}}+\bar{\mathbf{C}}\bar{\mathbf{D}}\bar{\mathbf{E}}+\bar{\mathbf{C}}\bar{\mathbf{C}}\bar{\mathbf{F}}+\bar{\mathbf{C}}\bar{\mathbf{C}}\bar{\mathbf{C}}\bar{\mathbf{C}}+\bar{\mathbf{D}}\bar{\mathbf{D}}\bar{\mathbf{D}}

\displaystyle+\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{D}}\bar{\mathbf{F}}+\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{E}}\bar{\mathbf{E}}+\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{C}}\bar{\mathbf{G}}+\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{C}}\bar{\mathbf{C}}\bar{\mathbf{D}}+\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{H}}+\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{D}}\bar{\mathbf{D}}

(4.8)

\displaystyle+\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{C}}\bar{\mathbf{E}}+\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{F}}+\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{D}}+\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{C}}\bar{\mathbf{C}}+\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{B}}\,.

For degree thirteen expansions, which involve
\{\bar{\mathbf{E}},\bar{\mathbf{I}}\}
,
\{\bar{\mathbf{F}},\bar{\mathbf{H}}\}
,
\{\bar{\mathbf{G}},\bar{\mathbf{G}}\}
, we have the following:

\displaystyle\{\bar{\mathbf{E}},\bar{\mathbf{I}}\}\sim\{\bar{\mathbf{F}},\bar{\mathbf{H}}\}\sim\{\bar{\mathbf{G}},\bar{\mathbf{G}}\}\sim\bar{\mathbf{D}}\bar{\mathbf{I}}

\displaystyle+\bar{\mathbf{E}}\bar{\mathbf{H}}+\bar{\mathbf{F}}\bar{\mathbf{G}}+\bar{\mathbf{D}}\bar{\mathbf{D}}\bar{\mathbf{E}}+\bar{\mathbf{C}}\bar{\mathbf{E}}\bar{\mathbf{E}}+\bar{\mathbf{C}}\bar{\mathbf{D}}\bar{\mathbf{F}}+\bar{\mathbf{C}}\bar{\mathbf{C}}\bar{\mathbf{G}}+\bar{\mathbf{C}}\bar{\mathbf{C}}\bar{\mathbf{C}}\bar{\mathbf{D}}+\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{E}}\bar{\mathbf{F}}

\displaystyle+\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{D}}\bar{\mathbf{G}}+\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{C}}\bar{\mathbf{H}}+\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{C}}\bar{\mathbf{D}}\bar{\mathbf{D}}+\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{C}}\bar{\mathbf{C}}\bar{\mathbf{E}}+\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{I}}+\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{D}}\bar{\mathbf{E}}+\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{C}}\bar{\mathbf{F}}

(4.9)

(4.13)

presented herein further demonstrates the significant reduction in the number of permissible terms achieved through the application of the grading method, prior to performing explicit calculations. In the following subsections, we elaborate on the precise formulas of the generators that are obtained via the grading method.

4.1.
The allowed monomials from the grading of Poisson brackets

We now study the specific grading assigned to each generator within the set
\mathcal{P}
. Additionally, we perform a detailed analysis of the grading associated with their respective Poisson brackets, using the terminology and definitions outlined in Subsection
2.3
.

Assume that
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{g}_{1}\oplus\mathfrak{g}_{2}\oplus\mathfrak{g}_{3}
is a semisimple Lie algebra with

\displaystyle[\mathfrak{g}_{1},\mathfrak{g}_{1}]

\displaystyle\,\subset\mathfrak{g}_{1},\quad[\mathfrak{g}_{2},\mathfrak{g}_{2}]\subset\mathfrak{g}_{2},\quad[\mathfrak{g}_{1},\mathfrak{g}_{2}]=\{0\},

(4.14)

\displaystyle[\mathfrak{g}_{1},\mathfrak{g}_{3}]

\displaystyle\,\subset\mathfrak{g}_{3},\quad[\mathfrak{g}_{2},\mathfrak{g}_{3}]\subset\mathfrak{g}_{3},\quad[\mathfrak{g}_{3},\mathfrak{g}_{3}]\subset\mathfrak{g}_{1}\oplus\mathfrak{g}_{2}.

Then
S(\mathfrak{g})\cong S(\mathfrak{g}_{1})\otimes S(\mathfrak{g}_{2})\otimes S(\mathfrak{g}_{3})
. A first step consists of knowing the grading of the non-trivial bracket of the generators of the commutant
S(\mathfrak{g})^{\mathfrak{g}_{1}\oplus\mathfrak{g}_{2}}.
Without loss of generality, assume that
\mathfrak{g}_{1}=\mathrm{span}\{x_{1},\ldots,x_{u}\}
,
\mathfrak{g}_{2}=\mathrm{span}\{x_{u+1},\ldots,x_{u+v}\}
, and
\mathfrak{g}_{3}=\mathrm{span}\{x_{u+v+1},\ldots,x_{u+v+w}\}
. Let
\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}=\mathfrak{g}_{1}\oplus\mathfrak{g}_{2}
be a subalgebra of
\mathfrak{g}
such that
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathfrak{g}}(d)
is a polynomial algebra consisting of
\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}
-invariant polynomials. We further assume that a monomial with degree
i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}
in
\mathcal{Q}_{i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}}\subset\mathcal{Q}_{\mathfrak{g}}(d)
has the form of

\displaystyle p^{i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}}=\underbrace{x_{1}^{a_{1}}\cdots x_{u}^{a_{u}}}_{\text{elements in $\mathfrak{g}_{1}$}}\underbrace{x_{u+1}^{a_{u+1}}\cdots x_{u+v}^{a_{u+v}}}_{\text{elements in $\mathfrak{g}_{2}$}}\underbrace{x_{u+v+1}^{a_{u+v+1}}\cdots x_{u+v+w}^{a_{u+v+w}}}_{\text{elements in $\mathfrak{g}_{3}$}},\quad\begin{matrix}a_{1}+\ldots+a_{u}=i_{1}\\
a_{u+1}+\ldots+a_{u+v}=i_{2},\\
a_{u+v+1}+\ldots+a_{u+v+w}=i_{3}\end{matrix}

\text{ with }a_{1},\ldots,a_{u+v+w}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}.
By definition, for any non-zero
p\in\mathcal{Q}_{i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}}
,
\mathcal{G}(p)=(i_{1},i_{2},i_{3}).

Lemma 4.1
.

Let
\mathfrak{g}^{*}
be its dual admitting the same relations as in
(
4.14
)
, and let
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathfrak{g}}(d)\subset S(\mathfrak{g})^{\mathfrak{g}_{1}\oplus\mathfrak{g}_{2}}.
For any non-zero indecomposable monomials
p\in\mathcal{Q}_{i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}}
and
q\in\mathcal{Q}_{i_{1}^{\prime}+i_{2}^{\prime}+i_{3}^{\prime}}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}\left(\{p,q\}\right)=(i_{1}+i_{1}^{\prime}-1,i_{2}+i_{2}^{\prime},i_{3}+i_{3}^{\prime})\tilde{+}(i_{1}+i_{1}^{\prime},i_{2}+i_{2}^{\prime}-1,i_{3}+i_{3}^{\prime})\tilde{+}\left\{\begin{matrix}(i_{1}+i_{1}^{\prime}+1,i_{2}+i_{2}^{\prime},i_{3}+i_{3}^{\prime}-2)&\text{ if }\text{ }[\mathfrak{g}_{3},\mathfrak{g}_{3}]\subset\mathfrak{g}_{1}\\
\\
(i_{1}+i_{1}^{\prime},i_{2}+i_{2}^{\prime}+1,i_{3}+i_{3}^{\prime}-2)&\text{ if }\text{ }[\mathfrak{g}_{3},\mathfrak{g}_{3}]\subset\mathfrak{g}_{2}.\end{matrix}\right.

Proof.

For any non-zero monomials
p\in\mathcal{Q}_{i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}}
and
q\in\mathcal{Q}_{i_{1}^{\prime}+i_{2}^{\prime}+i_{3}^{\prime}}
without loss of generality, we may write that
p=Y_{1}Y_{2}Y_{3}
and
q=Z_{1}Z_{2}Z_{3},
where

\displaystyle Y_{1}=x_{1}^{a_{1}}\cdots x_{u}^{a_{u}},\text{ }Y_{2}=x_{u+1}^{a_{u+1}}\cdots x_{u+v}^{a_{u+v}},\text{ }Y_{3}=x_{u+v+1}^{a_{u+v+1}}\cdots x_{u+v+w}^{a_{u+v+w}};

\displaystyle Z_{1}=x_{1}^{a_{1}^{\prime}}\cdots x_{u}^{a_{u}^{\prime}},\text{ }Z_{2}=x_{u+1}^{a_{u+1}^{\prime}}\cdots x_{u+v}^{a_{u+v}^{\prime}},\text{ }Z_{3}=x_{u+v+1}^{a_{u+v+1}^{\prime}}\cdots x_{u+v+w}^{a_{u+v+w}^{\prime}}.

Here
a_{1}+\ldots+a_{u}=i_{1},a_{u+1}+\ldots+a_{u+v}=i_{2}
,
a_{u+v+1}+\ldots+a_{u+v+w}=i_{3}
and
a_{1}^{\prime}+\ldots+a_{u}^{\prime}=i_{1}^{\prime},a_{u+1}^{\prime}+\ldots a_{u+v}^{\prime}=i_{2}^{\prime}
,
a_{u+v+1}^{\prime}+\ldots+a_{u+v+w}^{\prime}=i_{3}^{\prime}.
A direct computation shows that

(4.16)

\displaystyle\{p,q\}=\{Y_{1},q\}Y_{2}Y_{3}+Y_{1}\{Z_{2},q\}Z_{3}+Y_{1}Y_{2}\{Y_{3},q\}.

By assumption, the equation
(
4.16
)
becomes

(4.17)

\displaystyle\{p,q\}=

\displaystyle\,Y_{1}Y_{2}\{Y_{3},Z_{1}\}Z_{2}Z_{3}+Y_{1}Y_{2}\{Y_{3},Z_{2}\}Z_{1}Z_{3}+Y_{1}Y_{2}\{Y_{3},Z_{3}\}Z_{2}Z_{1}\,.

By definition, the grading of
\{p,q\}
in
(
4.17
)
is equal to the grading of each of the components. From the commutator relations,
\{A_{1},B_{1}\}=0
. We can then discard this term in
\eqref{eq:grading3}.
For the rest of the components in
(
4.17
)
, we compute them case by case. Starting from the term
Y_{1}Y_{2}\{Y_{3},Z_{1}\}Z_{2}Z_{3}
, a direct computation shows that

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}\left(Y_{1}Y_{2}\{Y_{3},Z_{1}\}Z_{2}Z_{3}\right)=

\displaystyle\,\mathcal{G}\left(\{Y_{3},Z_{1}\}\right)+\mathcal{G}(Z_{2})+\mathcal{G}(Z_{3})+\mathcal{G}(Y_{1})+\mathcal{G}(Y_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,(i_{1}-1,0,i_{3})+(i_{1}^{\prime},0,0)+(0,0,i_{3}^{\prime})+(0,i_{2},0)+(0,i_{2}^{\prime},0)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,(i_{1}+i_{1}^{\prime}-1,i_{2}+i_{2}^{\prime},i_{3}+i_{3}^{\prime}).

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}\left(Y_{1}Y_{2}\{Y_{3},Z_{2}\}Z_{1}Z_{3}\right)=

\displaystyle\,(i_{1}+i_{1}^{\prime},i_{2}+i_{2}^{\prime}-1,i_{3}+i_{3}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}\left(Y_{1}Y_{2}\{Y_{3},Z_{3}\}Z_{2}Z_{1}\right)=

\displaystyle\,\left\{\begin{matrix}(i_{1}+i_{1}^{\prime}+1,i_{2}+i_{2}^{\prime},i_{3}+i_{3}^{\prime}-2)&\text{ if }\text{ }[\mathfrak{g}_{3},\mathfrak{g}_{3}]\subset\mathfrak{g}_{1}\\
(i_{1}+i_{1}^{\prime},i_{2}+i_{2}^{\prime}+1,i_{3}+i_{3}^{\prime}-2)&\,\text{ if }\text{ }[\mathfrak{g}_{3},\mathfrak{g}_{3}]\subset\mathfrak{g}_{2}.\end{matrix}\right.

Summing all the terms together, we deduce the grading of
\{p,q\}.

∎

Remark 4.2
.

From the generators provided in
(
3.42
)
, we observe that

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}\left(\{p,q\}\right)=

\displaystyle\,(i_{1}+i_{1}^{\prime}-1,i_{2}+i_{2}^{\prime},i_{3}+i_{3}^{\prime})\tilde{+}(i_{1}+i_{1}^{\prime},i_{2}+i_{2}^{\prime}-1,i_{3}+i_{3}^{\prime})\tilde{+}(i_{1}+i_{1}^{\prime}+1,i_{2}+i_{2}^{\prime},i_{3}+i_{3}^{\prime}-2)

(4.18)

\displaystyle\,\tilde{+}(i_{1}+i_{1}^{\prime},i_{2}+i_{2}^{\prime}+1,i_{3}+i_{3}^{\prime}-2).

We next examine the grading of each generator within
\mathcal{P}.

Proposition 4.3
.

The grading of each generator in
\mathcal{P}
are given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}(\bar{b}_{1})=

\displaystyle\,(2,0,0),\quad\mathcal{G}(\bar{b}_{2})=(0,2,0),\quad\mathcal{G}(\bar{b}_{3})=(0,0,2);

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}(\bar{c}_{1})=

\displaystyle\,(1,1,1)\tilde{+}(0,0,3),\quad\mathcal{G}(\bar{C}_{2})=(1,1,1);

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}(\bar{d}_{1})=

\displaystyle\,(2,0,2)\tilde{+}(0,2,2)\tilde{+}(1,1,2)\tilde{+}(0,0,4);

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}(\bar{D}_{2})=

\displaystyle\,(1,1,2),\quad\mathcal{G}(\bar{D}_{3})=(0,2,2),\quad\mathcal{G}(\bar{D}_{4})=(0,0,4),\quad\mathcal{G}(\bar{E}_{1})=(1,1,3);

(4.19)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}(\bar{F}_{1})=

\displaystyle\,(2,1,3),\quad\hskip 2.84544pt\mathcal{G}(\bar{F}_{2})=(2,0,4),\quad\thinspace\mathcal{G}(\bar{F}_{3})=(1,2,3),\quad\mathcal{G}(\bar{F}_{4})=(0,2,4);

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}(\bar{G}_{1})=

\displaystyle\,(2,1,4),\quad\hskip 1.42271pt\mathcal{G}(\bar{G}_{2})=(1,2,4),\quad\mathcal{G}(\bar{H}_{1})=(2,1,5),\quad\mathcal{G}(\bar{H}_{2})=(1,2,5),

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}(\bar{I}_{1})=

\displaystyle\,(0,3,6),\hskip 5.69046pt\quad\mathcal{G}(\bar{I}_{2})=(3,0,6).

Remark 4.4
.

Using the generators delineated in equations
(
3.40
)
and
(
3.42
)
, we can determine that the homogeneous components of the generators denoted
\bar{c}_{1}
and
\bar{d}_{1}
are precisely constituted by the generators
\bar{C}_{2}
,
\bar{D}_{2}
,
\bar{D}_{3}
, and
\bar{D}_{4}
. We then represent these components in the subsequent manner:

(4.20)

\displaystyle\bar{c}_{1}=\bar{C}_{2}-\frac{2}{3}C^{(003)}\text{ and }\bar{d}_{1}=C^{(202)}-\bar{D}_{2}+\bar{D}_{3}-2\bar{D}_{4},

where
\mathcal{G}\left(C^{(003)}\right)=(0,0,3)
and
\mathcal{G}\left(C^{(202)}\right)=(2,0,2)
. As a consequence of these expressions, additional terms emerge within the grading of the Poisson bracket
\{p,q\}
. Consequently, in the initial calculation of the anticipated grading
\mathcal{G}(\{p,q\})
, we will determine the permissible terms consisting of
C^{(003)}
and
C^{(202)}
from each homogeneous grading. Then, under the change of generators, all the allowed generators in terms of
\bar{c}_{1}
and
\bar{d}_{1}
can be obtained. Clearly, this involves the further terms with the grading that do not match
\mathcal{G}(\{p,q\}).
This implies that the summation of these terms must equal the homogeneous terms
C^{(003)}
or
C^{(202)}
. This gives us some constraints on determining the coefficients. This point will become clearer in the following subsections when we will study explicit examples.

In the following, using Proposition
4.3
together with Lemma
4.1
, we shall elucidate the grading associated with each Poisson bracket in detail. Additionally, we will provide the gradings of all the compact forms, and demonstrate all allowable terms derived from the homogeneous gradings. As
\bar{b}_{1},\bar{b}_{2},\bar{b}_{3}
and
\bar{c}_{1}
are central elements, we will start with the degree
6
brackets.

4.2.
Expansions in the Degree
6
and
7
brackets

We examine the expansions of the compact forms
\{\bar{\textbf{C}},\bar{\textbf{D}}\}
,
\{\bar{\textbf{C}},\bar{\textbf{E}}\}
, and
\{\bar{\textbf{D}},\bar{\textbf{D}}\}
. We now present the explicit formula for the polynomial relations within these significant brackets, utilizing the grading for clarification. Initially, we examine the non-trivial bracket involving a non-central element from
\bar{C}_{2}
and
\bar{D}_{2},\bar{D}_{3},\bar{D}_{4}
. Through straightforward computation, it is obvious that

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}\left(\{\bar{C}_{2},\bar{D}_{2}\}\right)=

\displaystyle\,(1,2,3)\tilde{+}(2,1,3)\tilde{+}(3,2,1)\tilde{+}(2,3,1);

(4.21)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}\left(\{\bar{C}_{2},\bar{D}_{3}\}\right)=

\displaystyle\,(0,3,3)\tilde{+}(1,2,3)\tilde{+}(1,4,1)\tilde{+}(2,3,1);

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}\left(\{\bar{C}_{2},\bar{D}_{4}\}\right)=

\displaystyle\,(0,1,5)\tilde{+}(1,0,5)\tilde{+}(2,1,3)\tilde{+}(1,2,3)\,.

From the grading of each generator in
\eqref{eq:generatorsgrad},
we can exclude the terms
(2,3,1),(3,2,1)
,
(0,3,3),(1,4,1)
,
(0,1,5)
and
(1,0,5)
as there are no polynomials in
\textbf{Alg}\langle\mathcal{P}\rangle
with the grading above. Then the gradings in
(
4.21
)
become

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}\left(\{\bar{C}_{2},\bar{D}_{2}\}\right)=(1,2,3)\tilde{+}(2,1,3)=\mathcal{G}\left(\{\bar{C}_{2},\bar{D}_{4}\}\right)=\mathcal{G}\left(\{\bar{C}_{2},\bar{D}_{3}\}\right).

Hence, the allowed terms in each Poisson bracket above are given by

(4.22)

\displaystyle\left\{\bar{C}_{2},\bar{D}_{2}\right\}=\Gamma_{34}^{1}\bar{F}_{1}+\Gamma_{34}^{2}\bar{F}_{3},\text{ }\left\{\bar{C}_{2},\bar{D}_{3}\right\}=\Gamma_{34}^{3}\bar{F}_{1}+\Gamma_{34}^{4}\bar{F}_{3},\text{ }\left\{\bar{C}_{2},\bar{D}_{4}\right\}=\Gamma_{34}^{5}\bar{F}_{1}+\Gamma_{34}^{6}\bar{F}_{3}.

Here
\Gamma_{34}^{k}
, for
1\leq k\leq 6
, are some constants to be determined. Let us remark that the subindices
``34\textquotedblright
in the coefficients indicate the corresponding degree of the generators appearing in the Poisson brackets on the left hand side. We will use this notation throughout the paper. Back to the compact forms, at the degree six, the formal relations from
(
4.2
)
reduce to

(4.23)

\left\{\bar{\textbf{C}},\bar{\textbf{D}}\right\}\sim\bar{\textbf{F}}.

With the help of the relations in
(
3.6
)
, for degree-six expansions, we explicitly get:

(4.24)

\{\bar{C}_{2},\bar{D}_{2}\}=-2{\rm i}(\bar{F}_{1}+\bar{F}_{3})\,,\qquad\{\bar{C}_{2},\bar{D}_{3}\}=-2{\rm i}\bar{F}_{3}\,,\qquad\{\bar{C}_{2},\bar{D}_{4}\}=0\,.

Now, we determine the expected polynomials in the degree-seven brackets. This involves the brackets in the compact forms of
\{\bar{\textbf{C}},\bar{\textbf{E}}\}
and
\{\bar{\textbf{D}},\bar{\textbf{D}}\}.
We first consider the grading of the compact form
\{\bar{\textbf{C}},\bar{\textbf{E}}\}
. As
\bar{c}_{1}
is a central element, we only need to consider the expansion of the Poisson bracket
\{\bar{C}_{2},\bar{E}_{1}\}.
A direct calculation shows that

(4.25)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}\left(\{\bar{C}_{2},\bar{E}_{1}\}\right)=(1,2,4)\tilde{+}(2,1,4)\tilde{+}(3,2,2)\tilde{+}(2,3,2)\,.

It is clear that the allowed polynomials from the grading above are contained in
\eqref{eq:deg7}.
Hence,
\{\bar{C}_{2},\bar{E}_{1}\}=\Gamma_{35}^{1}\bar{G}_{1}+\Gamma_{35}^{2}\bar{G}_{2}.
On the other hand, the grading from the compact form
\{\bar{\textbf{D}},\bar{\textbf{D}}\}
is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}\left(\{\bar{D}_{2},\bar{D}_{3}\}\right)=

\displaystyle\,(0,3,4)\tilde{+}(1,2,4)\tilde{+}(2,3,2)\tilde{+}(1,4,2);

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}\left(\{\bar{D}_{3},\bar{D}_{4}\}\right)=

\displaystyle\,(0,1,6)\tilde{+}(1,2,4)\tilde{+}(0,3,4)\,.

Degree-seven Berezin brackets
\{\bar{\textbf{D}},\bar{\textbf{D}}\}
read explicitly as follows:

(4.26)

\{\bar{D}_{2},\bar{D}_{3}\}=2\{\bar{D}_{3},\bar{D}_{4}\}=4{\rm i}\bar{G}_{2}\,,\qquad\{\bar{D}_{2},\bar{D}_{4}\}=2\{\bar{E}_{1},\bar{C}_{2}\}=2{\rm i}\bigl(\bar{G}_{1}+\bar{G}_{2}\bigl)\,.

These relations conclude all the degree-seven Berezin brackets.

4.3.
Expansions in the degree
8
brackets

We now determine the non-trivial brackets with degree
8.
This involves the brackets in the compact forms of
\{\bar{\textbf{C}},\bar{\textbf{F}}\}
and
\{\bar{\textbf{D}},\bar{\textbf{E}}\}.
We first consider the grading of all the terms in
\{\bar{\textbf{C}},\bar{\textbf{F}}\}.
A direct computation shows that

(4.27)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}\left(\{\bar{C}_{2},\bar{F}_{1}\}\right)=

\displaystyle\,(2,2,4)\tilde{+}(3,1,4)\tilde{+}(4,2,2)\tilde{+}(3,3,2);

(4.28)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}\left(\{\bar{C}_{2},\bar{F}_{2}\}\right)=

\displaystyle\,(2,1,5)\tilde{+}(3,0,5)\tilde{+}(4,1,3)\tilde{+}(3,2,3);

(4.29)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}\left(\{\bar{C}_{2},\bar{F}_{3}\}\right)=

\displaystyle\,(1,3,4)\tilde{+}(2,2,4)\tilde{+}(3,3,2)\tilde{+}(2,4,2);

(4.30)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}\left(\{\bar{C}_{2},\bar{F}_{4}\}\right)=

\displaystyle\,(0,3,5)\tilde{+}(1,2,4)\tilde{+}(2,3,3)\tilde{+}(1,4,3).

From Proposition
4.3
, the allowed polynomials in each homogeneous grading of
(
4.27
)
, are given by

\displaystyle(2,2,4):=

\displaystyle\,\left\{\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{2}\bar{b}_{3}^{2},\text{ }\bar{C}_{2}^{2}\bar{b}_{3},\text{ }\bar{D}_{2}^{2},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{3}\bar{D}_{3},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{2}\bar{D}_{4},\text{ }\bar{C}_{2}\bar{E}_{1},\text{ }\bar{b}_{2}\bar{F}_{2},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{F}_{4},\text{ }C^{(202)}\bar{D}_{3},\text{ }\bar{b}_{2}\bar{b}_{3}C^{(202)}\right\},

\displaystyle(3,1,4):=

\displaystyle\,\left\{\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{3}\bar{D}_{2},\text{ }C^{(202)}\bar{D}_{2},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{c}_{2}C^{(003)}\right\},

\displaystyle(4,2,2):=

\displaystyle\,\left\{\bar{b}_{1}^{2}\bar{b}_{2}\bar{b}_{3},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{C}_{2}^{2},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}^{2}\bar{D}_{3},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{2}C^{(202)}\right\},

\displaystyle(3,3,2):=

\displaystyle\,\left\{\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{2}\bar{D}_{2}\right\}.

Here, the permissible polynomials are coming from the homogeneous gradings. As discussed in Remark
4.4
, we simply replace the non-central elements
C^{(202)}
and
C^{(003)}
by
\bar{d}_{1}
and
\bar{c}_{1}
respectively. We then easily deduce that the expected polynomials, under the new choice of the generators, are given by

\displaystyle\,\bar{b}_{1}\bar{F}_{4},\text{ }\bar{b}_{2}\bar{F}_{2},\text{ }\bar{C}_{2}\bar{E}_{1},\text{ }\bar{d}_{1}\bar{D}_{3},\text{ }\bar{D}_{2}\bar{D}_{3},\text{ }\bar{D}_{3}^{2},\text{ }\bar{D}_{3}\bar{D}_{4},\text{ }\bar{D}_{2}^{2},\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{2}\bar{D}_{4},

\displaystyle\,\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{3}\bar{D}_{3},\text{ }\bar{b}_{2}\bar{b}_{3}\bar{d}_{1},\text{ }\bar{b}_{2}\bar{b}_{3}\bar{D}_{2},\text{ }\bar{b}_{2}\bar{b}_{3}\bar{D}_{3},\text{ }\bar{b}_{2}\bar{b}_{3}\bar{D}_{4},\text{ }\bar{b}_{3}\bar{C}_{2}^{2},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{2}\bar{b}_{3}^{2},

\displaystyle\,\bar{d}_{1}\bar{D}_{2},\text{ }\bar{D}_{2}\bar{D}_{4},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{3}\bar{D}_{2},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{c}_{1}\bar{C}_{2},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{C}_{2}^{2},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}^{2}\bar{D}_{3},

\displaystyle\,\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{2}\bar{d}_{1},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{2}\bar{D}_{2},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{2}\bar{D}_{3},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}^{2}\bar{b}_{2}\bar{b}_{3}.

In what follows, we will perform a calculation of the total number of expected generators derived from the compact forms to various degrees. This serves to demonstrate the manner in which the grading technique can effectively streamline the preliminary computational processes. Considering the next case, the polynomials from the homogeneous grading in
(
4.28
)
are

\displaystyle(2,1,5):=

\displaystyle\,\left\{\bar{b}_{3}\bar{F}_{1},\bar{H}_{1}\right\},\text{ }(3,0,5):=\emptyset,\text{ }(4,1,3):=\left\{\bar{b}_{1}\bar{F}_{1}\right\}\text{ and }(3,2,3):=\emptyset.

From the discussion above, we may write

\displaystyle\{\bar{C}_{2},\bar{F}_{1}\}=

\displaystyle\Gamma_{36}^{1}\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{2}\bar{b}_{3}^{2}+\Gamma_{36}^{2}\bar{C}_{2}^{2}\bar{b}_{3}+\Gamma_{36}^{3}\bar{D}_{2}^{2}+\Gamma_{36}^{4}\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{3}\bar{D}_{3}+\Gamma_{36}^{5}\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{2}\bar{D}_{4}+\Gamma_{36}^{6}\bar{C}_{2}\bar{E}_{1}+\Gamma_{36}^{7}\bar{b}_{2}\bar{F}_{2}

\displaystyle\,+\Gamma_{36}^{8}\bar{b}_{1}\bar{F}_{4}+\Gamma_{36}^{9}\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{3}\bar{D}_{2}+\Gamma_{36}^{10}\bar{b}_{1}^{2}\bar{b}_{2}\bar{b}_{3}+\Gamma_{36}^{11}\bar{b}_{1}\bar{C}_{2}^{2}+\Gamma_{36}^{12}\bar{b}_{1}^{2}\bar{D}_{3}+\Gamma_{36}^{13}\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{2}\bar{D}_{2}

(4.31)

\displaystyle\,+\underbrace{\left(\Gamma_{36}^{14}\bar{b_{1}}\bar{c}_{1}\bar{C}_{2}+\Gamma_{36}^{15}\bar{b_{1}}\bar{C}_{2}^{2}+\Gamma_{36}^{16}\bar{d}_{1}\bar{D}_{2}+\ldots+\Gamma_{36}^{26}\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{2}\bar{d}_{1}\right)}_{\text{New terms under the change of the generators }};

\displaystyle\{\bar{C}_{2},\bar{F}_{2}\}=

\displaystyle\,\Gamma_{36}^{27}\bar{b}_{3}\bar{F}_{1}+\Gamma_{36}^{28}\bar{H}_{1}+\Gamma_{36}^{29}\bar{b}_{1}\bar{F}_{1}.

Here
\Gamma_{36}^{k}
, for
1\leq k\leq 29
, are constant coefficients. According to Remark
4.4
, to ensure that the grading of the left-hand side of the given expansion aligns with the grading associated with the bracket
\{\bar{C}_{2},\bar{F}_{1}\}
, we need to implement certain constraints in the new terms mentioned in
(
4.31
)
. For instance, there exists some
\alpha\in\mathbb{C}
such that
\left(\Gamma_{36}^{14}\bar{c}_{1}+\Gamma_{36}^{15}\bar{C}_{2}\right)\bar{C}_{2}\bar{b_{1}}=\alpha\bar{C}_{2}\bar{b_{1}}C^{(003)}
. This condition guarantees consistent grading between the constructs involved. With the help of the classical analog of the relations of
\mathfrak{su}(4)
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\{\bar{C}_{2},\bar{F}_{1}\}

\displaystyle=\,\frac{{\rm i}}{4}\bigl(-4\bar{b}_{3}\bar{C}_{2}^{2}+8\bar{b}_{2}\bar{b}_{3}\underbrace{(\bar{d}_{1}+\bar{D}_{2}-\bar{D}_{3}+2\bar{D}_{4})}_{\text{equals to $C^{(202)}$}}+2\bar{D}_{2}\underbrace{(\bar{d}_{1}+2\bar{D}_{2}-\bar{D}_{3}+2\bar{D}_{4})}_{\text{equals to $C^{(202)}+\bar{D}_{2}$}}+4\bar{C}_{2}\bar{E}_{1}-8\bar{b}_{2}\bar{F}_{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 25.6073pt+\bar{b}_{1}\bigl(\underbrace{(\bar{c}_{1}-\bar{C}_{2})}_{\text{equals to $-\frac{2}{3}C^{(003)}$}}\bar{C}_{2}+4\bar{b}_{3}\bar{D}_{3}+4\bar{b}_{2}(\bar{D}_{4}-\bar{b}_{3}^{2})-4\bar{F}_{4}\bigl)\bigl)\,,

(4.32)

\displaystyle\{\bar{C}_{2},\bar{F}_{2}\}

\displaystyle=\,-2{\rm i}\bar{H}_{1}\,.

Using a similar argument, we can deduce the allowed monomials from the brackets in
(
4.29
)
and
(
4.30
)
. The rest of the Poisson brackets in the compact form
\{\bar{\textbf{C}},\bar{\textbf{F}}\}
are then given by

\displaystyle\{\bar{C}_{2},\bar{F}_{3}\}

\displaystyle=\frac{{\rm i}}{4}\bigl(2\bar{D}_{2}(\bar{D}_{2}+\bar{D}_{3})+4\bar{C}_{2}(\bar{E}_{1}-\bar{b}_{3}\bar{C}_{2})+\bar{b}_{2}\bigl((\bar{c}_{1}-\bar{C}_{2})\bar{C}_{2}+4\bar{b}_{3}(\bar{d}_{1}+\bar{D}_{2}-\bar{D}_{3}+2\bar{D}_{4})-4\bar{F}_{2}\bigl)

\displaystyle\hskip 25.6073pt+\bar{b}_{1}\bigl(8\bar{b}_{3}\bar{D}_{3}+4\bar{b}_{2}(\bar{D}_{4}-\bar{b}_{3}^{2})-8\bar{F}_{4}\bigl)\bigl)\,,

(4.33)

\displaystyle\{\bar{C}_{2},\bar{F}_{4}\}

\displaystyle=-2{\rm i}\bar{H}_{2}\,.

Additionally, consider all the gradings in the compact form
\{\bar{\textbf{D}},\bar{\textbf{E}}\}
. Since
\bar{d}_{1}
is a central element, we will start from
\bar{D}_{2}.
A direct calculation shows that

(4.34)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}(\{\bar{D}_{2},\bar{E}_{1}\})=

\displaystyle\,(1,2,5)\tilde{+}(2,1,5)\tilde{+}(3,2,3)\tilde{+}(2,3,3);

(4.35)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}(\{\bar{D}_{3},\bar{E}_{1}\})=

\displaystyle\,(0,3,5)\tilde{+}(1,2,5)\tilde{+}(2,3,3)\tilde{+}(1,4,3);

(4.36)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}(\{\bar{D}_{4},\bar{E}_{1}\})=

\displaystyle\,(0,1,7)\tilde{+}(1,0,7)\tilde{+}(2,1,5)\tilde{+}(1,2,5).

The way of choosing permissible polynomials will be the same as presented above. Hence, we only provide one of these cases. Consider the grading from the bracket
\{\bar{D}_{2},\bar{E}_{1}\},
the allowed polynomials in each component are

(4.37)

\displaystyle(1,2,5):=\left\{\bar{H}_{2}\right\},\quad(2,1,5):=\left\{\bar{b}_{3}\bar{F}_{1},\bar{H}_{1}\right\},\quad(3,2,3):=\left\{\bar{b}_{1}\bar{F}_{3}\right\},\quad(2,3,3):=\left\{\bar{b}_{2}\bar{F}_{1}\right\}.

Then the expected generators in the Poisson bracket
(
4.34
)
are given by

\displaystyle\{\bar{D}_{2},\bar{E}_{1}\}=\Gamma_{45}^{1}\bar{H}_{1}+\Gamma_{45}^{2}\bar{H}_{2}+\left(\Gamma_{45}^{3}\bar{b}_{3}+\Gamma_{45}^{4}\bar{b}_{2}\right)\bar{F}_{1}+\Gamma_{45}^{5}\bar{b}_{1}\bar{F}_{3}.

Here,
\Gamma_{45}^{k}
are constant coefficients for all
1\leq k\leq 5
. In the explicit calculation, the explicit values of the coefficients are
\Gamma_{45}^{1}=\Gamma_{45}^{2}=2{\rm i}
,
\Gamma_{45}^{3}=0
, and
\Gamma_{45}^{4}=\Gamma_{45}^{5}=\frac{{\rm i}}{2}
, leading to

(4.38)

\displaystyle\{\bar{D}_{2},\bar{E}_{1}\}

\displaystyle={\rm i}\left(2\bar{H}_{1}+2\bar{H}_{2}+\frac{1}{2}(\bar{b}_{2}\bar{F}_{1}+\bar{b}_{1}\bar{F}_{3})\right)\,.

Analogously, from the grading
(
4.35
)
and
(
4.36
)
, we also find

\displaystyle\{\bar{D}_{3},\bar{E}_{1}\}

\displaystyle={\rm i}\bigl(-2\bar{H}_{2}+\frac{1}{2}\bar{b}_{2}\bar{F}_{1}+2\bar{b}_{3}\bar{F}_{3}\bigl)\,,

(4.39)

\displaystyle\{\bar{D}_{4},\bar{E}_{1}\}

\displaystyle={\rm i}\bigl(-2\bar{H}_{2}-2\bar{H}_{1}+\bar{b}_{3}(\bar{F}_{1}+\bar{F}_{3})\bigl)\,.

This conclude the computations of the Poisson brackets up to degree eight.

4.4.
Expansions in the degree
9
brackets

We now focus on the compact form corresponding to the degree
9
bracket relations. The compact forms are
\{\bar{\textbf{C}},\bar{\textbf{G}}\}
and
\{\bar{\textbf{D}},\bar{\textbf{F}}\}
. We start by analyzing the compact form
\{\bar{\textbf{D}},\bar{\textbf{F}}\}
. The gradings in the term
\{\bar{D}_{2},\bar{F}_{s}\}
for all
1\leq s\leq 4
are given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}\left(\{\bar{D}_{2},\bar{F}_{1}\}\right)=

\displaystyle\,(2,2,5)\tilde{+}(3,1,5)\tilde{+}(4,2,3)\tilde{+}(3,3,3);

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}\left(\{\bar{D}_{2},\bar{F}_{2}\}\right)=

\displaystyle\,(2,1,6)\tilde{+}(3,0,6)\tilde{+}(4,1,4)\tilde{+}(3,2,4);

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}\left(\{\bar{D}_{2},\bar{F}_{3}\}\right)=

\displaystyle\,(1,3,5)\tilde{+}(2,2,5)\tilde{+}(3,3,3)\tilde{+}(2,4,3);

(4.40)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}\left(\{\bar{D}_{2},\bar{F}_{4}\}\right)=

\displaystyle\,(1,2,6)\tilde{+}(0,3,6)\tilde{+}(2,3,4)\tilde{+}(1,4,4).

Moreover, for the Poisson brackets
\{\bar{D}_{3},\bar{F}_{s}\}
and
\{\bar{D}_{4},\bar{F}_{s}\}
are given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}\left(\{\bar{D}_{3},\bar{F}_{1}\}\right)=

\displaystyle\,(1,3,5)\tilde{+}(2,2,5)\tilde{+}(3,3,3)\tilde{+}(2,4,3);

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}\left(\{\bar{D}_{3},\bar{F}_{2}\}\right)=

\displaystyle\,(1,2,6)\tilde{+}(2,1,6)\tilde{+}(3,2,4)\tilde{+}(2,3,4);

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}\left(\{\bar{D}_{3},\bar{F}_{3}\}\right)=

\displaystyle\,(0,4,5)\tilde{+}(1,3,5)\tilde{+}(2,4,3)\tilde{+}(1,5,3);

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}\left(\{\bar{D}_{3},\bar{F}_{4}\}\right)=

\displaystyle\,(0,3,6)\tilde{+}(1,4,4)\tilde{+}(0,5,4);

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}\left(\{\bar{D}_{4},\bar{F}_{1}\}\right)=

\displaystyle\,(1,1,7)\tilde{+}(2,0,7)\tilde{+}(3,1,5)\tilde{+}(2,2,5);

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}\left(\{\bar{D}_{4},\bar{F}_{2}\}\right)=

\displaystyle\,(1,0,8)\tilde{+}(3,0,6)\tilde{+}(2,1,6);

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}\left(\{\bar{D}_{4},\bar{F}_{3}\}\right)=

\displaystyle\,(0,2,7)\tilde{+}(1,1,7)\tilde{+}(2,2,5)\tilde{+}(1,3,5);

(4.41)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}\left(\{\bar{D}_{4},\bar{F}_{4}\}\right)=

\displaystyle\,(0,1,8)\tilde{+}(1,2,6)\tilde{+}(0,3,6).

Note that, using Lemma
4.1
, the grading of
\{\bar{D}_{3},\bar{F}_{4}\}
contains the homogeneous grading
(-1,4,6)
. It is clear that, from Proposition
4.3
, there is no generator or product of generators in
\mathcal{P}
satisfying such homogeneous grading. Hence, we will omit the terms like that.

The procedure for finding the admissible monomials is, in fact, the same as before. Thus, we outline the permissible terms within certain gradings. Consider the grading in the bracket
\{\bar{D}_{2},\bar{F}_{1}\}.
Note that there will be extra polynomial terms by considering the change of the generators. This can be seen as follows. The admissible monomials for each homogeneous gradings are given by

\displaystyle(2,2,5):=

\displaystyle\,\left\{\bar{D}_{2}\bar{E}_{1},\text{ }\bar{b}_{3}\bar{C}_{2}\bar{D}_{2},\text{ }C^{(003)}\bar{C}_{2}^{2},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}C^{(003)}\bar{D}_{3},\text{ }\bar{b}_{2}C^{(003)}C^{(202)},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{2}\bar{b}_{3}C^{(003)}\right\};

(4.42)

\displaystyle(3,1,5):=

\displaystyle\,\left\{\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{3}\bar{E}_{1},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{3}^{2}\bar{C}_{2},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{C}_{2}\bar{D}_{4},\text{ }\bar{C}_{2}\bar{F}_{2},\text{ }C^{(202)}\bar{E}_{1},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}C^{(003)}\bar{D}_{2},\text{ }\bar{b}_{3}\bar{C}_{2}C^{(202)}\right\};

\displaystyle(4,2,3):=

\displaystyle\,\left\{\bar{b}_{1}\bar{D}_{2}\bar{C}_{2},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}^{2}\bar{b}_{2}C^{(003)}\right\};

\displaystyle(3,3,3):=

\displaystyle\,\left\{\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{2}\bar{E}_{1},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{2}\bar{b}_{3}\bar{C}_{2},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{C}_{2}\bar{D}_{3},\text{ }\bar{C}_{2}^{3},\text{ }\bar{b}_{2}C^{(202)}\bar{C}_{2}\right\}.

As mentioned before, the permissible monomials that arise from these gradings can be described as follows:

\displaystyle\,\bar{D}_{2}\bar{E}_{1},\bar{b}_{1}\bar{c}_{1}\bar{D}_{3},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{C}_{2}\bar{D}_{3}\text{ },\bar{b}_{2}\bar{c}_{1}\bar{d}_{1},\text{ }\bar{b}_{2}\bar{c}_{1}\bar{D}_{2},\text{ }\bar{b}_{2}\bar{c}_{1}\bar{D}_{3},\text{ }\bar{b}_{2}\bar{c}_{1}\bar{D}_{4},\bar{b}_{2}\bar{C}_{2}\bar{d}_{1},

\displaystyle\,\bar{b}_{2}\bar{C}_{2}\bar{D}_{2},\bar{b}_{2}\bar{C}_{2}\bar{D}_{3},\text{ }\bar{b}_{2}\bar{C}_{2}\bar{D}_{4},\text{ }\bar{b}_{3}\bar{C}_{2}\bar{D}_{2},\text{ }\bar{c}_{1}\bar{C}_{2}^{2},\text{ }\bar{C}_{2}^{3},\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{2}\bar{b}_{3}\bar{c}_{1},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{C}_{2}\bar{D}_{4},

\displaystyle\,\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{2}\bar{b}_{3}\bar{C}_{2},\bar{C}_{2}\bar{F}_{2},\bar{d}_{1}\bar{E}_{1},\text{ }\bar{D}_{3}\bar{E}_{1},\bar{D}_{4}\bar{E}_{1},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{3}\bar{E}_{1},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{c}_{1}\bar{D}_{2},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{C}_{2}\bar{D}_{2},

\displaystyle\,\bar{b}_{3}\bar{C}_{2}\bar{d}_{1},\text{ }\bar{b}_{3}\bar{C}_{2}\bar{D}_{3},\bar{b}_{3}\bar{C}_{2}\bar{D}_{4},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{3}^{2}\bar{C}_{2},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}^{2}\bar{b}_{2}\bar{c}_{1},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}^{2}\bar{b}_{2}\bar{C}_{2},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{2}\bar{E}_{1}.

From this, we conclude that there are some coefficients
\Gamma_{46}^{k}
, with
1\leq k\leq 31
, such that

\displaystyle\{\bar{D}_{2},\bar{F}_{1}\}=

\displaystyle\,\Gamma_{46}^{1}\bar{D}_{2}\bar{E}_{1}+\ldots+\Gamma_{46}^{30}\bar{C}_{2}^{3}+\Gamma_{46}^{31}\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{2}\bar{E}_{1}.

Again, these coefficients can be determined via the classical analog of the relations of
\mathfrak{su}(4)
and the constraints from the homogeneous gradings. Eventually, we deduce that

\displaystyle\{\bar{D}_{2},\bar{F}_{1}\}

\displaystyle=\frac{{\rm i}}{8}\bigl(\bar{b}_{1}^{2}\bar{b}_{2}(\bar{c}_{1}-\bar{C}_{2})+\bar{b}_{1}\bigl(4\bar{b}_{2}\bar{b}_{3}(\bar{c}_{1}-\bar{C}_{2})-8\bar{b}_{3}^{2}\bar{C}_{2}-2(\bar{c}_{1}-2\bar{C}_{2})(\bar{D}_{2}+2\bar{D}_{3})+8\bar{C}_{2}\bar{D}_{4}-4\bar{b}_{2}\bar{E}_{1}\bigl)

\displaystyle\qquad+4\bigl(-3\bar{C}_{2}^{3}+2\bar{c}_{1}\bigl(\bar{C}_{2}^{2}-\bar{b}_{2}(\bar{d}_{1}+\bar{D}_{2}-\bar{D}_{3}+2\bar{D}_{4})\bigl)-2\bar{D}_{2}\bar{E}_{1}+\bar{C}_{2}(3\bar{b}_{2}(\bar{d}_{1}+\bar{D}_{2}-\bar{D}_{3}+2\bar{D}_{4})

(4.43)

\displaystyle\qquad+\bar{b}_{3}(4\bar{d}_{1}+6\bar{D}_{2}-4\bar{D}_{3}+8\bar{D}_{4})-4\bar{F}_{2})\bigl)\bigl)\,.

Similarly, the rest of the degree-nine relations are provided as follows:

\displaystyle\{\bar{D}_{2},\bar{F}_{2}\}

\displaystyle={\rm i}((-\bar{c}_{1}+2\bar{C}_{2})\bar{F}_{1}+(\bar{b}_{1}+4\bar{b}_{3})\bar{G}_{1})\,,

\displaystyle\{\bar{D}_{2},\bar{F}_{3}\}

\displaystyle=\frac{{\rm i}}{8}\bigl(\bar{b}_{1}\bigl((\bar{b}_{2}^{2}+4\bar{b}_{2}\bar{b}_{3})(\bar{c}_{1}-\bar{C}_{2})-4(2\bar{c}_{1}-3\bar{C}_{2})\bar{D}_{3}-4\bar{b}_{2}\bar{E}_{1}\bigl)+4(2\bar{c}_{1}-3\bar{C}_{2})\bar{C}_{2}^{2}-8\bar{D}_{2}\bar{E}_{1}

\displaystyle\hskip 25.6073pt+8\bar{b}_{3}\bar{C}_{2}(\bar{D}_{2}+2\bar{D}_{3})-2\bar{b}_{2}\bigl(4\bar{b}_{3}^{2}\bar{C}_{2}^{2}+(\bar{c}_{1}-2\bar{C}_{2})(2\bar{d}_{1}+3\bar{D}_{2}-2\bar{D}_{3})+4(\bar{c}_{1}-3\bar{C}_{2})\bar{D}_{4}\bigl)

\displaystyle\hskip 25.6073pt-16\bar{C}_{2}\bar{F}_{4}\bigl)\,,

(4.44)

\displaystyle\{\bar{D}_{2},\bar{F}_{4}\}

\displaystyle={\rm i}\bigl((-\bar{c}_{1}+2\bar{C}_{2})\bar{F}_{3}+(\bar{b}_{2}+4\bar{b}_{3})\bar{G}_{2}\bigl)\,.

On the other hand, using the grading in (
4.41
), we can determine the allowed polynomials in the Poisson brackets
\{\bar{D}_{3},\bar{F}_{s}\}
and
\{\bar{D}_{4},\bar{F}_{s}\}
for all
1\leq s\leq 4.
Taking into account the Poisson relations of
\mathfrak{su}^{*}(4)
given in (
3.6
), we then deduce

\displaystyle\{\bar{D}_{3},\bar{F}_{1}\}

\displaystyle=\frac{\rm i}{2}\bigl(((\bar{c}_{1}-2\bar{C}_{2})\bar{C}_{2}+\bar{b}_{1}\bar{D}_{3}+\bar{b}_{2}(\bar{d}_{1}+\bar{D}_{2}-\bar{D}_{3}+2\bar{D}_{4}))\bar{C}_{2}-(\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{2}-2\bar{D}_{2})\bar{E}_{1}\bigl)\,,

\displaystyle\{\bar{D}_{3},\bar{F}_{2}\}

\displaystyle={\rm i}\bar{C}_{2}\bar{F}_{1}\,,

\displaystyle\{\bar{D}_{3},\bar{F}_{3}\}

\displaystyle=\frac{{\rm i}}{8}\bigl(\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{2}^{2}(\bar{c}_{1}-\bar{C}_{2})-2\bar{b}_{2}\bar{c}_{1}\bar{D}_{2}-4\bar{b}_{2}\bar{C}_{2}(2\bar{b}_{3}^{2}-\bar{D}_{2}-2\bar{D}_{4})+16((\bar{b}_{3}\bar{C}_{2}+\bar{E}_{1})\bar{D}_{3}-2\bar{C}_{2}\bar{F}_{4})\bigl)\,,

\displaystyle\{\bar{D}_{3},\bar{F}_{4}\}

\displaystyle={\rm i}\bigl(-4\bar{I}_{1}+\bar{b}_{2}\bar{G}_{2}\bigl)\,,

\displaystyle\{\bar{D}_{4},\bar{F}_{1}\}

\displaystyle=\frac{{\rm i}}{4}\bigl(\bar{C}_{2}^{3}+2\bigl(\bar{b}_{2}(\bar{c}_{1}-\bar{C}_{2})+2\bar{E}_{1}\bigl)(\bar{d}_{1}+\bar{D}_{2}-\bar{D}_{3}+2\bar{D}_{4})-2\bar{b}_{3}\bar{C}_{2}\bar{D}_{2}-\bar{c}_{1}\bar{C}_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 25.6073pt-\bar{b}_{1}(\bar{c}_{1}-\bar{C}_{2})(\bar{b}_{2}\bar{b}_{3}-\bar{D}_{3})+4(\bar{D}_{2}\bar{E}_{1}-\bar{C}_{2}\bar{F}_{2})\bigl)\,,

\displaystyle\{\bar{D}_{4},\bar{F}_{2}\}

\displaystyle={\rm i}\bigl(-2(\bar{I}_{2}+\bar{b}_{3}\bar{G}_{1})+\frac{1}{2}(\bar{c}_{1}-\bar{C}_{2})\bar{F}_{1}\bigl)\,,

\displaystyle\{\bar{D}_{4},\bar{F}_{3}\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}(\{\bar{D}_{3},\bar{F}_{1}\}+\{\bar{D}_{3},\bar{F}_{3}\}-\{\bar{D}_{2},\bar{F}_{3}\})\,,

(4.45)

\displaystyle\{\bar{D}_{4},\bar{F}_{4}\}

\displaystyle={\rm i}\bigl(\frac{1}{2}(\bar{c}_{1}-\bar{C}_{2})\bar{F}_{3}-2\bar{b}_{3}\bar{G}_{2}-2\bar{I}_{1}\bigl)\,.

These computations clarify how the absence of a certain grading affects the admissible polynomials in the Poisson brackets. From now on, our analysis concentrates on presenting data on the allowable generators that arise from homogeneous gradings, selected from one case associated with each compact form. This approach will enable us to examine the differences that arise by transitioning from
C^{(003)}
and
C^{(202)}
to
\bar{c}_{1}
and
\bar{d}_{1}
, respectively.

Now, for the grading in the compact form
\{\bar{\textbf{C}},\bar{\textbf{G}}\},
which includes the Poisson brackets
\{\bar{C}_{2},\bar{G}_{1}\}
and
\{\bar{C}_{2},\bar{G}_{2}\}.
A direct computation shows that

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}\left(\{\bar{C}_{2},\bar{G}_{1}\}\right)=(2,2,5)\tilde{+}(3,1,5)\tilde{+}(4,2,3)\tilde{+}(4,3,3);

(4.46)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}\left(\{\bar{C}_{2},\bar{G}_{2}\}\right)=(1,3,5)\tilde{+}(2,2,5)\tilde{+}(3,3,3)\tilde{+}(2,4,3).

Here, we present the allowed polynomials from each homogeneous grading in
\mathcal{G}\left(\{\bar{C}_{2},\bar{G}_{2}\}\right).
A direct calculations shows that

\displaystyle(1,3,5):=

\displaystyle\,\left\{\bar{C}_{2}\bar{F}_{4},\text{ }\bar{D}_{3}\bar{E}_{1},\text{ }\bar{b}_{2}\bar{b}_{3}^{2}\bar{C}_{2},\text{ }\bar{b}_{2}\bar{b}_{3}\bar{E}_{1},\text{ }\bar{b}_{2}\bar{C}_{2}\bar{D}_{4},\text{ }\bar{b}_{3}\bar{C}_{2}\bar{D}_{3},\text{ }\bar{b}_{2}C^{(003)}\bar{D}_{2}\right\};

(4.47)

\displaystyle(2,2,5):=

\displaystyle\,\left\{\bar{D}_{2}\bar{E}_{1},\text{ }\bar{b}_{3}\bar{C}_{2}\bar{D}_{2},\text{ }\bar{D}_{2}\bar{E}_{1},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}C^{(003)}\bar{D}_{3},\text{ }\bar{b}_{2}C^{(003)}C^{(202)},\text{ }C^{(003)}\bar{C}_{2}^{2},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{2}\bar{b}_{3}C^{(003)}\right\};

\displaystyle(3,3,3):=

\displaystyle\,\left\{\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{2}\bar{E}_{1},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{C}_{2}\bar{D}_{3},\text{ }\bar{C}_{2}^{3},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{2}\bar{b}_{3}\bar{C}_{2},\text{ }\bar{b}_{2}\bar{C}_{2}C^{(202)}\right\};

\displaystyle(2,4,3):=

\displaystyle\,\left\{\bar{b}_{2}\bar{C}_{2}\bar{D}_{2},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{2}^{2}C^{(003)}\right\}.

Hence, together with the polynomials found in
(
4.47
)
, all the admissible polynomials in terms of the extended terms
\bar{c}_{1}
and
\bar{d}_{1}
are given by

\displaystyle\,\bar{C}_{2}\bar{F}_{4},\text{ }\bar{D}_{3}\bar{E}_{1},\text{ }\bar{b}_{2}\bar{b}_{3}\bar{E}_{1},\text{ }\bar{b}_{2}\bar{c}_{1}\bar{D}_{2},\text{ }\bar{b}_{2}\bar{C}_{2}\bar{D}_{2},\text{ }\bar{b}_{2}\bar{C}_{2}\bar{D}_{4},\text{ }\bar{b}_{3}\bar{C}_{2}\bar{D}_{3},\text{ }\bar{b}_{2}\bar{b}_{3}^{2}\bar{C}_{2},\text{ }\bar{b}_{3}\bar{C}_{2}\bar{D}_{2},

\displaystyle\,\bar{D}_{2}\bar{E}_{1},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{c}_{1}\bar{D}_{3},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{C}_{2}\bar{D}_{3},\text{ }\bar{b}_{2}\bar{c}_{1}\bar{d}_{1},\text{ }\bar{b}_{2}\bar{c}_{1}\bar{D}_{3},\text{ }\bar{b}_{2}\bar{c}_{1}\bar{D}_{4},\text{ }\bar{b}_{2}\bar{C}_{2}\bar{d}_{1},\text{ }\bar{b}_{2}\bar{C}_{2}\bar{D}_{3},

\displaystyle\,\bar{c}_{1}\bar{C}_{2}^{2},\text{ }\bar{C}_{2}^{3},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{2}\bar{b}_{3}\bar{c}_{1},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{2}\bar{b}_{3}\bar{C}_{2},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{2}\bar{E}_{1},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{2}^{2}\bar{c}_{1},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{2}^{2}\bar{C}_{2}.

There are
24
coefficients such that
\{\bar{C}_{2},\bar{G}_{2}\}=\Gamma_{37}^{1}\bar{C}_{2}\bar{F}_{4}+\ldots+\Gamma_{37}^{24}\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{2}^{2}\bar{C}_{2}.
Moreover, from
(
4.5
)
, the compact forms
\{\bar{\textbf{C}},\bar{\textbf{G}}\}
and
\{\bar{\textbf{D}},\bar{\textbf{F}}\}
have the same expansion. The grading method indicates that the permissible terms can be reduced from
65
to
24.
Together with the information above, after direct inspection we can further conclude that
\{\bar{C}_{2},\bar{G}_{2}\}=\{\bar{D}_{4},\bar{F}_{3}\}\,.
Similarly, we also deduce that

(4.48)

\displaystyle\{\bar{C}_{2},\bar{G}_{1}\}=\{\bar{D}_{4},\bar{F}_{1}\}\,.

This concludes all the expansion in degree nine Poisson brackets.

4.5.
Expansions in the degree
10
brackets

For the case of brackets having degree
10
, the reduced forms can be grouped into three distinct compact forms:
\{\bar{\textbf{C}},\bar{\textbf{H}}\},

\{\bar{\textbf{D}},\bar{\textbf{G}}\},
and
\{\bar{\textbf{E}},\bar{\textbf{F}}\}
. We first examine the compact form of type
\{\bar{\textbf{C}},\bar{\textbf{H}}\}
, which includes the Poisson brackets
\{\bar{C}_{2},\bar{H}_{1}\}
and
\{\bar{C}_{2},\bar{H}_{2}\}.
The gradations assigned to each Poisson brackets are as follows

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}\left(\{\bar{C}_{2},\bar{H}_{1}\}\right)=(2,2,6)\tilde{+}(3,1,6)\tilde{+}(4,2,4)\tilde{+}(3,3,4);

(4.49)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}\left(\{\bar{C}_{2},\bar{H}_{2}\}\right)={(1,3,6)}\tilde{+}(2,2,6)\tilde{+}(3,3,4)\tilde{+}(2,4,4);

As an example, the allowed monomials in each homogeneous grading for the second bracket are

\displaystyle(1,3,6):=

\displaystyle\,\{\bar{D}_{2}\bar{F}_{4},\text{ }\bar{b}_{2}\bar{D}_{2}\bar{D}_{4},\text{ }\bar{b}_{3}\bar{D}_{2}\bar{D}_{3},\text{ }\bar{b}_{2}\bar{b}_{3}^{2}\bar{D}_{2},\text{ }\bar{b}_{2}C^{(003)}\bar{E}_{1},\text{ }\bar{b}_{2}\bar{b}_{3}C^{(003)}\bar{C}_{2},\text{ }C^{(003)}\bar{C}_{2}\bar{D}_{3}\};

\displaystyle(2,2,6):=

\displaystyle\,\{\bar{D}_{3}\bar{F}_{2},\text{ }\bar{E}_{1}^{2},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{3}\bar{F}_{4},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{D}_{3}\bar{D}_{4},\text{ }\bar{b}_{2}\bar{b}_{3}\bar{F}_{2},\text{ }\bar{b}_{3}\bar{C}_{2}\bar{E}_{1},\text{ }\bar{b}_{3}\bar{D}_{2}^{2},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{2}\bar{b}_{3}^{3},\text{ }\bar{C}_{2}^{2}\bar{D}_{4},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{2}\bar{b}_{3}\bar{D}_{4},\text{ }\bar{b}_{3}^{2}\bar{C}_{2}^{2},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{3}^{2}\bar{D}_{3},

\displaystyle\,\bar{b}_{2}C^{(202)}\bar{D}_{4},\text{ }C^{(202)}\bar{F}_{4},\text{ }\bar{b}_{3}C^{(202)}\bar{D}_{3},\text{ }C^{(003)}\bar{C}_{2}\bar{D}_{2},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{2}\left(C^{(003)}\right)^{2},\text{ }\bar{b}_{2}\bar{b}_{3}^{2}C^{(202)}\};

\displaystyle(3,3,4):=

\displaystyle\,\{\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{2}\bar{b}_{3}\bar{D}_{2},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{D}_{2}\bar{D}_{3},\text{ }\bar{C}_{2}^{2}\bar{D}_{2},\text{ }\bar{b}_{2}C^{(202)}\bar{D}_{2},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{2}C^{(003)}\bar{C}_{2}\};

\displaystyle(2,4,4):=

\displaystyle\,\{\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{2}\bar{F}_{4},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{D}_{3}^{2},\text{ }\bar{b}_{2}^{2}\bar{F}_{2},\text{ }\bar{b}_{2}\bar{C}_{2}\bar{E}_{1},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{2}^{2}\bar{b}_{3}^{2},\text{ }\bar{b}_{2}\bar{D}_{2}^{2},\text{ }\bar{C}_{2}^{2}\bar{D}_{3},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{2}^{2}\bar{D}_{4},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{2}\bar{b}_{3}\bar{D}_{3},\text{ }\bar{b}_{2}\bar{b}_{3}\bar{C}_{2}^{2},\text{ }\bar{b}_{2}C^{(202)}\bar{D}_{3},\text{ }\bar{b}_{2}^{2}\bar{b}_{3}C^{(202)}\}.

Again, the allowed polynomials underline the corresponding one with the homogeneous gradings, and we deduce that all the allowed polynomials in the expansion
\{\bar{C}_{2},\bar{H}_{2}\}
are

\displaystyle\,\bar{D}_{2}\bar{F}_{4},\text{ }\bar{b}_{2}\bar{c}_{1}\bar{E}_{1},\text{ }\bar{b}_{2}\bar{C}_{2}\bar{E}_{1},\text{ }\bar{b}_{2}\bar{D}_{2}\bar{D}_{4},\bar{b}_{3}\bar{D}_{2}\bar{D}_{3},\text{ }\bar{c}_{1}\bar{C}_{2}\bar{D}_{3},\text{ }\bar{C}_{2}^{2}\bar{D}_{3},\text{ }\bar{b}_{2}\bar{b}_{3}^{2}\bar{D}_{2},\text{ }\bar{b}_{2}\bar{b}_{3}\bar{c}_{1}\bar{C}_{2},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{2}\bar{c}_{1}^{2},\text{ }\bar{b}_{2}\bar{D}_{3}^{2},

\displaystyle\,\bar{b}_{2}\bar{b}_{3}\bar{C}_{2}^{2},\text{ }\bar{d}_{1}\bar{F}_{4},\text{ }\bar{D}_{3}\bar{F}_{4},\text{ }\bar{D}_{4}\bar{F}_{4},\text{ }\bar{D}_{3}\bar{F}_{2},\text{ }\bar{E}_{1}^{2},\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{3}\bar{F}_{4},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{D}_{3}\bar{D}_{4},\text{ }\bar{b}_{2}\bar{b}_{3}\bar{F}_{2},\text{ }\bar{b}_{2}\bar{d}_{1}\bar{D}_{4},\text{ }\bar{b}_{2}\bar{D}_{3}\bar{D}_{4},

\displaystyle\,\bar{b}_{2}\bar{D}_{4}^{2},\text{ }\bar{b}_{3}\bar{C}_{2}\bar{E}_{1},\text{ }\bar{b}_{3}\bar{d}_{1}\bar{D}_{3},\text{ }\bar{b}_{3}\bar{D}_{3}^{2},\text{ }\bar{b}_{3}\bar{D}_{3}\bar{D}_{4},\text{ }\bar{b}_{3}\bar{D}_{2}^{2},\text{ }\bar{c}_{1}\bar{C}_{2}\bar{D}_{2},\text{ }\bar{C}_{2}^{2}\bar{D}_{2},\text{ }\bar{C}_{2}^{2}\bar{D}_{4},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{2}\bar{b}_{3}\bar{D}_{4},

\displaystyle\,\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{2}\bar{c}_{1}\bar{C}_{2},\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{2}\bar{C}_{2}^{2},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{3}^{2}\bar{D}_{3},\text{ }\bar{b}_{2}\bar{b}_{3}^{2}\bar{d}_{1},\text{ }\bar{b}_{2}\bar{b}_{3}^{2}\bar{D}_{3},\text{ }\bar{b}_{2}\bar{b}_{3}^{2}\bar{D}_{4},\text{ }\text{ }\bar{b}_{3}^{2}\bar{C}_{2}^{2},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{2}\bar{b}_{3}^{3},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{D}_{2}\bar{D}_{3},

\displaystyle\,\bar{b}_{2}\bar{d}_{1}\bar{D}_{2},\text{ }\bar{b}_{2}\bar{D}_{2}^{2},\bar{b}_{2}\bar{D}_{2}\bar{D}_{3},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{2}\bar{b}_{3}\bar{D}_{2},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{2}\bar{c}_{1}\bar{C}_{2},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{2}\bar{F}_{4},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{D}_{3}^{2},\text{ }\bar{b}_{2}^{2}\bar{F}_{2},\text{ }\bar{b}_{2}\bar{d}_{1}\bar{D}_{3},

\displaystyle\,\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{2}^{2}\bar{D}_{4},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{2}\bar{b}_{3}\bar{D}_{3},\text{ }\bar{b}_{2}^{2}\bar{b}_{3}\bar{d}_{1},\text{ }\bar{b}_{2}^{2}\bar{b}_{3}\bar{D}_{2},\text{ }\bar{b}_{2}^{2}\bar{b}_{3}\bar{D}_{3},\text{ }\bar{b}_{2}^{2}\bar{b}_{3}\bar{D}_{4},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{2}^{2}\bar{b}_{3}^{2}.

The maximal possible count of coefficients denoted as
\Gamma_{38}^{1},\ldots,\Gamma_{38}^{57}
reaches
57
, wherein the expression
\{\bar{C}_{2},\bar{H}_{2}\}
can be represented as
\Gamma_{38}^{1}\bar{D}_{2}\bar{F}_{4}+\ldots+\Gamma_{38}^{57}\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{2}^{2}\bar{b}_{3}^{2}.
By meticulously evaluating and identifying each of these coefficients, we derive

\displaystyle\{\bar{C}_{2},\bar{H}_{2}\}

\displaystyle=-\frac{{\rm i}}{32}\bigl(-24\bar{b}_{3}^{2}\bar{C}_{2}^{2}-4\bar{b}_{3}(\bar{D}_{2}^{2}-16\bar{C}_{2}\bar{E}_{1}+4\bar{b}_{2}\bar{F}_{2})+8\bigl(\bar{C}_{2}^{2}(\bar{D}_{2}+\bar{D}_{4})+\bar{b}_{2}\bar{C}_{2}\bar{E}_{1}

\displaystyle\hskip 35.56593pt+2\bar{b}_{2}\bar{D}_{4}(\bar{d}_{1}+\bar{D}_{2}-\bar{D}_{3}+2\bar{D}_{4})-2\bar{E}_{1}^{2}-\bar{c}_{1}(\bar{C}_{2}\bar{D}_{2}+\bar{b}_{2}\bar{E}_{1})+2\bar{D}_{3}\bar{F}_{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 35.56593pt-2(3\bar{d}_{1}+4\bar{D}_{2}-3\bar{D}_{3}+6\bar{D}_{4})\bar{F}_{4}\bigl)+\bar{b}_{1}\bigl(\bar{b}_{2}(8\bar{b}_{3}^{3}-(\bar{c}_{1}-\bar{C}_{2})^{2}-8\bar{b}_{3}\bar{D}_{4})

\displaystyle\hskip 35.56593pt+16\bar{b}_{3}(\bar{F}_{4}-\bar{b}_{3}\bar{D}_{3})\bigl)\bigl)\,.

With a similar approach, the other bracket relation in degree-ten expansions of the compact form
\{\bar{\textbf{C}},\bar{\textbf{H}}\}
reads explicitly:

\displaystyle\{\bar{C}_{2},\bar{H}_{1}\}

\displaystyle=\frac{{\rm i}}{16}\bigl(2\bar{C}_{2}\bigl((12\bar{b}_{3}^{2}-3\bar{D}_{2}-4\bar{D}_{4})\bar{C}_{2}+3\bar{c}_{1}\bar{D}_{2}-24\bar{b}_{3}\bar{E}_{1}\bigl)+8(\bar{D}_{2}+4\bar{D}_{3})\bar{F}_{2}

(4.50)

\displaystyle\hskip 25.6073pt+\bar{b}_{1}\bigl(\bar{b}_{2}(\bar{c}_{1}-\bar{C}_{2})^{2}-8\bar{D}_{3}(\bar{b}_{3}^{2}+\bar{D}_{4})+4(\bar{c}_{1}-\bar{C}_{2})\bar{E}_{1}+16\bar{b}_{3}\bar{F}_{4}\bigl)\bigl)\,.

Now, in the compact form
\{\bar{\textbf{D}},\bar{\textbf{G}}\}
, since

is the central element, we will consider the Poisson brackets

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}\left(\{\bar{D}_{2},\bar{G}_{1}\}\right)=(2,2,6)\tilde{+}(3,1,6)\tilde{+}(4,2,4)\tilde{+}(3,3,4);

\displaystyle\{\bar{D}_{2},\bar{G}_{1}\}

\displaystyle=\frac{{\rm i}}{8}\bigl(2\bar{b}_{1}^{2}(2\bar{F}_{4}-2\bar{b}_{3}\bar{D}_{3}+\bar{b}_{2}(\bar{b}_{3}^{2}-\bar{D}_{4}))+2(\bar{c}_{1}\bar{C}_{2}-2\bar{C}_{2}^{2})(2\bar{d}_{1}+\bar{D}_{2}-2\bar{D}_{3})-8\bar{b}_{3}^{2}\bar{C}_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+8(\bar{c}_{1}\bar{C}_{2}-3\bar{C}_{2}^{2})\bar{D}_{4}+32\bar{b}_{3}\bar{C}_{2}\bar{E}_{1}-16\bar{E}_{1}^{2}+16\bar{D}_{3}\bar{F}_{2}+8\bar{b}_{2}^{2}(\bar{F}_{2}-\bar{b}_{3}(\bar{d}_{1}+\bar{D}_{2}-\bar{D}_{3}+2\bar{D}_{4})

\displaystyle\qquad+2\bar{b}_{2}(2\bar{d}_{1}^{2}+\bar{D}_{2}^{2}+16\bar{D}_{2}\bar{D}_{4}+4\bar{d}_{1}(\bar{D}_{2}+4\bar{D}_{4})-2(\bar{D}_{3}-2\bar{D}_{4})(\bar{D}_{3}+6\bar{D}_{4})-8\bar{C}_{2}\bar{E}_{1}

(4.52)

\displaystyle\qquad-4(\bar{c}_{1}-2\bar{C}_{2})\bar{E}_{1}+\bar{b}_{2}(\bar{c}_{1}\bar{C}_{2}-\bar{C}_{2}^{2}-8\bar{b}_{3}\bar{D}_{3}-4\bar{F}_{2}+8\bar{F}_{4})\bigl)\bigl)\,.

Eventually, with the same process above, the rest of the additional degree-ten bracket relations are obtained:

\displaystyle\{\bar{D}_{2},\bar{G}_{2}\}

\displaystyle=-\frac{{\rm i}}{8}\bigl(8\bar{b}_{3}^{2}\bar{C}_{2}^{2}+2(\bar{c}_{1}\bar{C}_{2}-2\bar{C}_{2}^{2})(\bar{D}_{2}-2\bar{D}_{3})+\bar{b}_{2}\bar{D}_{2}^{2}+8\bar{C}_{2}^{2}\bar{D}_{4}+4(\bar{b}_{2}\bar{c}_{1}-2\bar{b}_{2}\bar{C}_{2})\bar{E}_{1}+16\bar{E}_{1}^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+4\bar{b}_{3}(\bar{b}_{2}^{2}\bigl(\bar{d}_{1}+\bar{D}_{2}-\bar{D}_{3}+2\bar{D}_{4})-8\bar{C}_{2}\bar{E}_{1}\bigl)-4(\bar{b}_{2}^{2}-4\bar{D}_{3})\bar{F}_{2}-16(\bar{d}_{1}+\bar{D}_{2}-\bar{D}_{3}+2\bar{D}_{4})\bar{F}_{4}

\displaystyle\qquad+\bar{b}_{1}\bigl(2\bar{b}_{2}^{2}(\bar{D}_{4}-\bar{b}_{3}^{2})-4\bar{D}_{3}(\bar{D}_{3}+4\bar{D}_{4})+4(\bar{b}_{2}+4\bar{b}_{3})\bar{F}_{4}+\bar{b}_{2}(\bar{C}_{2}^{2}-\bar{c}_{1}\bar{C}_{2})\bigl)\bigl)\,,

\displaystyle\{\bar{D}_{3},\bar{G}_{1}\}

\displaystyle=\frac{{\rm i}}{8}\bigl(2\bar{b}_{1}\bigl(2\bar{D}_{3}^{2}+\bar{b}_{2}^{2}(\bar{b}_{3}^{2}-\bar{D}_{4})-2\bar{b}_{2}\bar{F}_{4}\bigl)-\bar{b}_{2}\bigl(4\bar{b}_{3}(\bar{c}_{1}-\bar{C}_{2})\bar{C}_{2}+\bar{D}_{2}^{2}-4\bar{c}_{1}\bar{E}_{1}\bigl)

\displaystyle\qquad+4\bar{b}_{2}^{2}\bigl(\bar{F}_{2}-\bar{b}_{3}(\bar{d}_{1}+\bar{D}_{2}-\bar{D}_{3}+2\bar{D}_{4})\bigl)+4\bigl((\bar{c}_{1}\bar{C}_{2}-2\bar{C}_{2}^{2}-2\bar{b}_{3}\bar{D}_{2})\bar{D}_{3}+4\bar{D}_{2}\bar{F}_{4}\bigl)\bigl)\,,

\displaystyle\{\bar{D}_{4},\bar{G}_{1}\}

\displaystyle=\frac{{\rm i}}{4}\bigl(4\bar{b}_{3}^{2}\bar{C}_{2}^{2}+(\bar{c}_{1}\bar{C}_{2}-\bar{C}_{2}^{2})\bar{D}_{2}-4(\bar{b}_{2}\bar{d}_{1}+\bar{b}_{2}\bar{D}_{2}-\bar{b}_{2}\bar{D}_{3})\bar{D}_{4}-8\bar{b}_{2}\bar{D}_{4}^{2}+8\bar{E}_{1}^{2}+4(\bar{D}_{2}-\bar{D}_{3})\bar{F}_{2}

\displaystyle\qquad-\bar{b}_{1}(\bar{c}_{1}-\bar{C}_{2})(\bar{b}_{3}\bar{C}_{2}-2\bar{E}_{1})-2\bar{b}_{3}\bigl(\bar{D}_{2}(\bar{d}_{1}+\bar{D}_{2}-\bar{D}_{3}+2\bar{D}_{4})+6\bar{C}_{2}\bar{E}_{1}-2\bar{b}_{2}\bar{F}_{2}\bigl)

(4.53)

\displaystyle\{\bar{D}_{4},\bar{G}_{2}\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}(\{\bar{D}_{3},\bar{G}_{1}\}+\{\bar{D}_{3},\bar{G}_{2}\}-\{\bar{D}_{2},\bar{G}_{2}\})\,.

\{\bar{\textbf{E}},\bar{\textbf{F}}\}
for which we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}(\{\bar{E}_{1},\bar{F}_{1}\})=

\displaystyle\,(2,2,6)\tilde{+}(3,1,6)\tilde{+}(3,3,4)\tilde{+}(4,2,4);

\displaystyle(2,2,6):=

\displaystyle\,\left.\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{2}\left(C^{(003)}\right)^{2},\text{ }\bar{b}_{2}\bar{b}_{3}^{2}C^{(202)},\text{ }C^{(003)}\bar{C}_{2}\bar{D}_{2},\text{ }C^{(202)}\bar{F}_{4},\text{ }\bar{b}_{2}C^{(202)}\bar{D}_{4},\text{ }\bar{b}_{3}C^{(202)}\bar{D}_{3}\right\};

\displaystyle(3,1,6):=

\displaystyle\,\left\{\bar{D}_{2}\bar{F}_{2},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{D}_{2}\bar{D}_{4},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{3}^{2}\bar{D}_{2},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}C^{(003)}\bar{E}_{1},\text{ }C^{(003)}\bar{C}_{2}C^{(202)},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{3}C^{(003)}\bar{C}_{2},\text{ }\bar{b}_{3}C^{(202)}\bar{D}_{2}\right\}

\displaystyle(3,3,4):=

\displaystyle\,\left\{\bar{b}_{1}\bar{D}_{2}\bar{D}_{3},\text{ }\bar{C}_{2}^{2}\bar{D}_{2},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{2}\bar{b}_{3}\bar{D}_{2},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{2}C^{(003)}\bar{C}_{2},\text{ }\bar{b}_{2}C^{(202)}\bar{D}_{2}\right\};

\displaystyle(4,2,4):=

\displaystyle\,\left\{\bar{b}_{1}^{2}\bar{F}_{4},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{2}\bar{F}_{2},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{C}_{2}\bar{E}_{1},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{d}_{1}\bar{D}_{3},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{D}_{2}^{2},\text{ }\bar{b}_{2}\bar{d}_{1}^{2},\text{ }\bar{C}_{2}^{2}\bar{d}_{1},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}^{2}\bar{b}_{2}\bar{D}_{4},\bar{b}_{1}^{2}\bar{b}_{3}\bar{D}_{3},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{2}\bar{b}_{3}\bar{d}_{1},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{3}\bar{C}_{2}^{2},\text{ }\bar{b}_{1}^{2}\bar{b}_{2}\bar{b}_{3}^{2}\right\}.

After considering the change of generators, there are

predicted generators such that
\{\bar{E}_{1},\bar{F}_{1}\}=\Gamma_{56}^{1}\bar{d}_{1}\bar{F}_{4}+\ldots+\Gamma_{56}^{71}\bar{b}_{1}^{2}\bar{b}_{2}\bar{b}_{3}^{2}.

are constant coefficients that, once determined, lead us to the following result:

\displaystyle\{\bar{E}_{1},\bar{F}_{1}\}

\displaystyle=\frac{{\rm i}}{16}\bigl(16\bar{b}_{3}^{2}\bar{C}_{2}^{2}+4(\bar{c}_{1}\bar{C}_{2}-2\bar{C}_{2}^{2})\bar{d}_{1}+2(4\bar{c}_{1}\bar{C}_{2}-5\bar{C}_{2}^{2})\bar{D}_{2}-4(\bar{c}_{1}\bar{C}_{2}-2\bar{C}_{2}^{2})\bar{D}_{3}-16\bar{b}_{3}\bar{C}_{2}\bar{E}_{1}

\displaystyle\qquad+8(\bar{c}_{1}\bar{C}_{2}-4\bar{C}_{2}^{2})\bar{D}_{4}+2\bar{b}_{2}\bigl(2\bar{d}_{1}^{2}+4\bar{d}_{1}\bar{D}_{2}+\bar{D}_{2}^{2}-2\bar{D}_{3}^{2}+8(\bar{d}_{1}+\bar{D}_{2})\bar{D}_{4}+8\bar{D}_{4}^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+4\bar{C}_{2}(\bar{b}_{3}\bar{C}_{2}-2\bar{E}_{1})\bigl)-16\bar{E}_{1}^{2}+8\bar{D}_{2}\bar{F}_{2}+8\bar{b}_{2}^{2}\bigl(\bar{F}_{2}-\bar{b}_{3}(\bar{d}_{1}+\bar{D}_{2}-\bar{D}_{3}+2\bar{D}_{4})\bigl)

\displaystyle\qquad+32(\bar{d}_{1}+\bar{D}_{2}-\bar{D}_{3}+2\bar{D}_{4})\bar{F}_{4}+\bar{b}_{1}\bigl(4\bar{b}_{2}^{2}(\bar{b}_{3}^{2}-\bar{D}_{4})+2\bar{D}_{3}(-4\bar{b}_{3}^{2}+2\bar{d}_{1}+3\bar{D}_{2}+8\bar{D}_{4})

(4.55)

\displaystyle\qquad+\bar{b}_{2}(\bar{c}_{1}^{2}-\bar{C}_{2}^{2}-8\bar{b}_{3}\bar{D}_{3}-4\bar{F}_{2}+8\bar{F}_{4})\bigl)\bigl)\,.

We then list the rest of the expansions:

\displaystyle\{\bar{E}_{1},\bar{F}_{2}\}

\displaystyle=\frac{{\rm i}}{4}\bigl((2\bar{d}_{1}+\bar{D}_{2}+8\bar{D}_{4}-4\bar{b}_{3}^{2}-2\bar{b}_{2}\bar{b}_{3})\bar{F}_{1}-(4\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{3}+\bar{D}_{2})\bar{F}_{3}+2(\bar{c}_{1}-\bar{C}_{2})\bar{G}_{1}-2\bar{C}_{2}\bar{G}_{2}

\displaystyle\qquad-4(2\bar{d}_{1}+3\bar{D}_{2}-2\bar{D}_{3})\bar{D}_{4}\bigl)+8\bar{b}_{3}^{2}\bar{C}_{2}^{2}+4\bar{b}_{3}\bar{D}_{2}^{2}+4\bar{C}_{2}(\bar{c}_{1}\bar{D}_{2}+2(\bar{c}_{1}-2\bar{C}_{2})\bar{D}_{3}-6\bar{C}_{2}\bar{D}_{4})

\displaystyle\qquad-16\bar{E}_{1}^{2}-8(\bar{b}_{2}^{2}+2\bar{b}_{2}\bar{b}_{3}-6\bar{D}_{3})\bar{F}_{2}-16(\bar{d}_{1}-\bar{D}_{3}+2\bar{D}_{4})\bar{F}_{4}-\bar{b}_{1}\bigl(-8\bar{D}_{3}(2\bar{b}_{3}^{2}+\bar{D}_{3})

\displaystyle\qquad+4\bar{b}_{2}^{2}(\bar{b}_{3}^{2}-\bar{D}_{4})+16\bar{b}_{3}\bar{F}_{4}+\bar{b}_{2}(8\bar{b}_{3}^{3}-(\bar{c}_{1}-\bar{C}_{2})(\bar{c}_{1}+3\bar{C}_{2})-8\bar{b}_{3}(\bar{D}_{3}+\bar{D}_{4})+16\bar{F}_{4})\bigl)\bigl)\,,

(4.56)

\displaystyle\{\bar{E}_{1},\bar{F}_{4}\}

\displaystyle=-\frac{{\rm i}}{4}\bigl((4\bar{b}_{3}^{2}+\bar{D}_{2}-2\bar{D}_{3}-4\bar{D}_{4})\bar{F}_{3}-2(\bar{c}_{1}-\bar{C}_{2})\bar{G}_{2}+4\bar{b}_{2}(\bar{b}_{3}\bar{F}_{1}-\bar{H}_{1})\bigl)\,.

All the expansions above conclude the degree-ten Poisson brackets. We now look at the expansions for all the degree-eleven brackets.

4.6.
Expansions in the degree
11
brackets

In the context of degree eleven brackets, the corresponding compact forms can be identified as
\{\bar{\textbf{F}},\bar{\textbf{F}}\}
,
\{\bar{\textbf{C}},\bar{\textbf{I}}\}
,
\{\bar{\textbf{D}},\bar{\textbf{H}}\}
, and
\{\bar{\textbf{E}},\bar{\textbf{G}}\}
. Our initial focus is to compute all the expansions within the compact form
\{\bar{\textbf{F}},\bar{\textbf{F}}\}
, which includes the Poisson brackets

\displaystyle=\frac{{\rm i}}{16}\bigl(8\bar{b}_{3}\bar{C}_{2}(\bar{C}_{2}^{2}+\bar{b}_{3}\bar{D}_{2})+2(\bar{c}_{1}-3\bar{C}_{2})\bar{D}_{2}^{2}+4(2\bar{C}_{2}\bar{d}_{1}-\bar{c}_{1}\bar{D}_{2}+4\bar{C}_{2}\bar{D}_{2})\bar{D}_{3}

\displaystyle\qquad-40\bar{C}_{2}^{2}\bar{E}_{1}-16\bar{b}_{3}\bar{D}_{2}\bar{E}_{1}+32\bar{b}_{3}\bar{D}_{3}\bar{E}_{1}-2\bar{b}_{2}\bigl(\bar{C}_{2}^{3}+2\bar{b}_{3}\bar{C}_{2}(2\bar{d}_{1}+\bar{D}_{2}-2\bar{D}_{3}+4\bar{D}_{4})

\displaystyle\qquad+2(4\bar{b}_{3}^{2}-2\bar{d}_{1}-\bar{D}_{2}+2\bar{D}_{3}-8\bar{D}_{4})\bar{E}_{1}-\bar{c}_{1}(\bar{C}_{2}^{2}-4\bar{F}_{2})-8\bar{C}_{2}\bar{F}_{2}\bigl)-32\bar{E}_{1}\bar{F}_{4}

\displaystyle\qquad+\bar{b}_{1}\bigl(2\bar{b}_{2}^{2}\bar{b}_{3}(\bar{c}_{1}-\bar{C}_{2})+\bar{b}_{2}(12\bar{b}_{3}^{2}\bar{C}_{2}+\bar{c}_{1}(\bar{D}_{2}+8\bar{D}_{4})-\bar{C}_{2}(\bar{D}_{2}+20\bar{D}_{4})-8\bar{b}_{3}\bar{E}_{1})

\displaystyle\qquad-8(2\bar{b}_{3}\bar{C}_{2}\bar{D}_{3}+2\bar{c}_{1}\bar{F}_{4}-5\bar{C}_{2}\bar{F}_{4})\bigl)\bigl)\,,

\displaystyle\{\bar{D}_{3},\bar{H}_{1}\}

\displaystyle=\frac{{\rm i}}{16}\bigl(8(\bar{D}_{4}-\bar{b}_{3}^{2})\bar{C}_{2}\bar{D}_{2}-2\bar{c}_{1}\bar{D}_{2}^{2}+8(\bar{c}_{1}\bar{C}_{2}-2\bar{C}_{2}^{2}+2\bar{b}_{3}\bar{D}_{2}+\bar{b}_{1}\bar{D}_{3})\bar{E}_{1}+\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{2}\bigl(8\bar{b}_{3}^{2}\bar{C}_{2}+\bar{c}_{1}\bar{D}_{2}

\displaystyle\qquad-\bar{C}_{2}(\bar{D}_{2}+8\bar{D}_{4})-8\bar{b}_{3}\bar{E}_{1}\bigl)+8\bar{b}_{2}\bar{C}_{2}\bigl(\bar{b}_{3}(\bar{d}_{1}+\bar{D}_{2}-\bar{D}_{3}+2\bar{D}_{4})-2\bar{F}_{2}\bigl)\bigl)\,,

\displaystyle\{\bar{D}_{3},\bar{H}_{2}\}

\displaystyle=\frac{{\rm i}}{8}\bigl(\bar{b}_{2}^{2}(\bar{c}_{1}-\bar{C}_{2})(\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{3}-(\bar{d}_{1}+\bar{D}_{2}-\bar{D}_{3}+2\bar{D}_{4}))+2\bar{D}_{3}(12\bar{b}_{3}^{2}\bar{C}_{2}+\bar{c}_{1}\bar{D}_{2}-4\bar{C}_{2}\bar{D}_{4}-8\bar{b}_{3}\bar{E}_{1})

\displaystyle\qquad-\bar{b}_{2}(8\bar{b}_{3}^{3}\bar{C}_{2}+(\bar{c}_{1}-2\bar{C}_{2})(\bar{c}_{1}-\bar{C}_{2})\bar{C}_{2}+2\bar{b}_{3}\bar{c}_{1}\bar{D}_{2}-4\bar{b}_{3}\bar{C}_{2}(\bar{D}_{2}+2\bar{D}_{4})+2(\bar{D}_{2}+4\bar{D}_{4})\bar{E}_{1}

(4.62)

\displaystyle\qquad-8\bar{b}_{3}^{2}\bar{E}_{1})+32(\bar{E}_{1}-\bar{b}_{3}\bar{C}_{2})\bar{F}_{4}\bigl)\,,

and

\displaystyle\{\bar{D}_{4},\bar{H}_{1}\}

\displaystyle=\frac{{\rm i}}{16}\bigl(4\bar{C}_{2}^{2}\bar{E}_{1}-2\bar{C}_{2}\bar{D}_{2}(2\bar{d}_{1}+\bar{D}_{2}-2\bar{D}_{3})-8\bar{C}_{2}\bar{D}_{4}(\bar{d}_{1}+2\bar{D}_{2}-\bar{D}_{3})-16\bar{C}_{2}\bar{D}_{4}^{2}-8\bar{b}_{2}\bar{C}_{2}\bar{F}_{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+8\bar{b}_{3}^{2}\bar{C}_{2}(3\bar{d}_{1}+2\bar{D}_{2}-3\bar{D}_{3}+6\bar{D}_{4})+48\bar{E}_{1}\bar{F}_{2}+2\bar{c}_{1}\bigl(\bar{D}_{2}(2\bar{d}_{1}+\bar{D}_{2}-2\bar{D}_{3}+4\bar{D}_{4})-2\bar{C}_{2}\bar{E}_{1}

\displaystyle\qquad+4\bar{b}_{2}\bar{F}_{2}\bigl)-16\bar{b}_{3}\bigl((2\bar{d}_{1}+\bar{D}_{2}-2\bar{D}_{3}+4\bar{D}_{4})\bar{E}_{1}+2\bar{C}_{2}\bar{F}_{2}\bigl)-\bar{b}_{1}\bigl((8\bar{b}_{3}^{3}+\bar{c}_{1}^{2}-4\bar{b}_{2}\bar{D}_{4}

\displaystyle\qquad-2\bar{b}_{3}(\bar{D}_{2}+4\bar{D}_{4}))\bar{C}_{2}+\bar{C}_{2}^{3}+2\bar{b}_{3}\bar{c}_{1}\bar{D}_{2}+16(\bar{D}_{4}-\bar{b}_{3}^{2})\bar{E}_{1}+4\bar{C}_{2}\bar{F}_{4}-2\bar{c}_{1}(\bar{C}_{2}^{2}-2\bar{b}_{2}\bar{D}_{4}+2\bar{F}_{4})\bigl)\bigl)\,,

\displaystyle\{\bar{D}_{4},\bar{H}_{2}\}

\displaystyle=\frac{{\rm i}}{16}\bigl(2\bar{C}_{2}\bigl(\bar{D}_{2}^{2}-4\bar{b}_{3}^{2}(\bar{D}_{2}-3\bar{D}_{3})-2\bar{D}_{3}(\bar{D}_{2}+2\bar{D}_{4})\bigl)+4(\bar{C}_{2}^{2}+4\bar{b}_{3}(\bar{D}_{2}-2\bar{D}_{3})+12\bar{F}_{4})\bar{E}_{1}

\displaystyle\qquad-8\bar{b}_{1}\bar{C}_{2}\bar{F}_{4}-2\bar{c}_{1}(\bar{D}_{2}^{2}-2\bar{D}_{2}\bar{D}_{3}+2\bar{C}_{2}\bar{E}_{1}-4\bar{b}_{1}\bar{F}_{4})-32\bar{b}_{3}\bar{C}_{2}\bar{F}_{4}-\bar{b}_{2}\bigl(8\bar{b}_{3}^{2}\bar{C}_{2}+\bar{c}_{1}^{2}\bar{C}_{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+\bar{C}_{2}^{3}+2\bar{b}_{3}\bar{c}_{1}\bar{D}_{2}-4\bar{b}_{1}\bar{C}_{2}\bar{D}_{4}-2\bar{b}_{3}\bar{C}_{2}(\bar{D}_{2}+4\bar{D}_{4})-16\bar{b}_{3}^{2}\bar{E}_{1}+16\bar{D}_{4}\bar{E}_{1}+4\bar{C}_{2}\bar{F}_{2}

(4.63)

\displaystyle\qquad-2\bar{c}_{1}(\bar{C}_{2}^{2}-2\bar{b}_{1}\bar{D}_{4}+2\bar{F}_{2})\bigl)\bigl)\,.

Finally, we consider the compact form
\{\bar{\textbf{E}},\bar{\textbf{G}}\},
which contains only two Poisson brackets. The gradings of these terms are given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}(\{\bar{E}_{1},\bar{G}_{1}\})=

\displaystyle\,(2,2,7)\tilde{+}(3,1,7)\tilde{+}(4,2,5)\tilde{+}(3,3,5);

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}(\{\bar{E}_{1},\bar{G}_{2}\})=

\displaystyle\,(1,3,7)\tilde{+}(2,2,7)\tilde{+}(3,3,5)\tilde{+}(2,4,5).

Note that, from the computation above, we deduce that

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}(\{\bar{E}_{1},\bar{G}_{1}\})=\mathcal{G}\left(\{\bar{D}_{2},\bar{H}_{1}\}\right)\text{ and }\mathcal{G}(\{\bar{E}_{1},\bar{G}_{2}\})=\mathcal{G}\left(\{\bar{D}_{2},\bar{H}_{2}\}\right).

However, we observe that, even if the admissible polynomials from these gradings are the same, the coefficients in the Poisson brackets heavily depend on the structure tensor of the given Lie algebra. In this case, we deduce that the explicit expression in the expansion are

\displaystyle\{\bar{E}_{1},\bar{G}_{1}\}

\displaystyle=\frac{{\rm i}}{16}\bigl(\bar{C}_{2}(\bar{D}_{2}^{2}+4\bar{d}_{1}(\bar{D}_{2}+\bar{D}_{3})-4\bar{D}_{3}^{2}+8(\bar{D}_{2}-\bar{d}_{1}+2\bar{D}_{3})\bar{D}_{4}-16\bar{D}_{4}^{2})+8\bar{b}_{3}^{2}\bar{C}_{2}(\bar{d}_{1}-\bar{D}_{3}

\displaystyle\qquad+2\bar{D}_{4})+4\bar{E}_{1}\bigl(3\bar{c}_{1}\bar{C}_{2}-7\bar{C}_{2}^{2}+2\bar{b}_{2}(\bar{d}_{1}+\bar{D}_{2}-\bar{D}_{3}+2\bar{D}_{4})\bigl)+4(\bar{b}_{2}(3\bar{C}_{2}-2\bar{c}_{1})+4\bar{E}_{1})\bar{F}_{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+8\bar{b}_{3}\bigl(2\bar{C}_{2}^{3}+\bar{c}_{1}(\bar{b}_{2}(\bar{d}_{1}+\bar{D}_{2}-\bar{D}_{3}+2\bar{D}_{4})-\bar{C}_{2}^{2})+\bar{D}_{2}\bar{E}_{1}-2\bar{C}_{2}(\bar{b}_{2}(\bar{d}_{1}+\bar{D}_{2}-\bar{D}_{3}+2\bar{D}_{4})

\displaystyle\qquad+\bar{F}_{2})\bigl)-\bar{b}_{1}\bigl(\bar{c}_{1}^{2}\bar{C}_{2}-3\bar{c}_{1}\bar{C}_{2}^{2}+2\bar{C}_{2}^{3}+4(\bar{b}_{3}(5\bar{C}_{2}-\bar{c}_{1})-\bar{E}_{1})\bar{D}_{3}+\bar{b}_{2}\bigl(2\bar{b}_{3}^{2}(2\bar{c}_{1}-7\bar{C}_{2})

(4.64)

\displaystyle\qquad-(\bar{c}_{1}-\bar{C}_{2})(\bar{d}_{1}+2\bar{D}_{2}-\bar{D}_{3})+2\bar{D}_{4}(8\bar{C}_{2}-3\bar{c}_{1})+4\bar{b}_{3}\bar{E}_{1}\bigl)+4(\bar{c}_{1}-5\bar{C}_{2})\bar{F}_{4}\bigl)\bigl)\,

and

\displaystyle\{\bar{E}_{1},\bar{G}_{2}\}

\displaystyle=\frac{{\rm i}}{16}\bigl(8\bar{b}_{3}\bar{C}_{2}(2\bar{C}_{2}^{2}-\bar{c}_{1}\bar{C}_{2}-\bar{b}_{3}\bar{D}_{2})-3\bar{C}_{2}\bar{D}_{2}^{2}+4\bar{C}_{2}\bar{D}_{3}(\bar{d}_{1}+2(\bar{D}_{2}+\bar{b}_{3}^{2}))-4\bar{C}_{2}\bar{D}_{3}^{2}+8\bar{C}_{2}\bar{D}_{2}\bar{D}_{4}

\displaystyle\qquad+4(3\bar{c}_{1}\bar{C}_{2}-7\bar{C}_{2}^{2}+2\bar{b}_{3}\bar{D}_{2})\bar{E}_{1}+16(\bar{E}_{1}-\bar{b}_{3}\bar{C}_{2})\bar{F}_{4}-\bar{b}_{1}\bigl(-8\bar{D}_{3}(\bar{E}_{1}+\bar{b}_{3}(\bar{c}_{1}-2\bar{C}_{2}))

\displaystyle\qquad+\bar{b}_{2}(2\bar{b}_{3}^{2}(2\bar{c}_{1}-7\bar{C}_{2})-\bar{c}_{1}(\bar{D}_{2}+\bar{D}_{3}+4\bar{D}_{4})+\bar{C}_{2}(\bar{D}_{2}+\bar{D}_{3}+14\bar{D}_{4})+4\bar{b}_{3}\bar{E}_{1})

\displaystyle\qquad+4(2\bar{c}_{1}-3\bar{C}_{2})\bar{F}_{4}\bigl)-\bar{b}_{2}\bigl(\bar{c}_{1}^{2}\bar{C}_{2}-3\bar{c}_{1}\bar{C}_{2}^{2}+2\bar{C}_{2}^{3}-4(\bar{d}_{1}+\bar{D}_{2}-\bar{D}_{3}+2\bar{D}_{4})(\bar{E}_{1}+\bar{b}_{3}\bar{c}_{1})

(4.65)

\displaystyle\qquad+20\bar{C}_{2}(\bar{b}_{3}(\bar{d}_{1}+\bar{D}_{2}-\bar{D}_{3}+2\bar{D}_{4})-\bar{F}_{2})+4\bar{c}_{1}\bar{F}_{2}\bigl)\bigl)\,.

These expansions conclude the degree-eleven non-trivial Poisson brackets.

4.7.
Expansions in the degree
12
brackets

For the expansions associated with degree brackets
12
, the compact forms are characterized by distinct pairings of brackets, which include pairs such as
\{\bar{\mathbf{D}},\bar{\mathbf{I}}\}
,
\{\bar{\mathbf{E}},\bar{\mathbf{H}}\}
, and
\{\bar{\mathbf{F}},\bar{\mathbf{G}}\}
. We begin with the compact form represented by
\{\bar{\mathbf{D}},\bar{\mathbf{I}}\}
. Thorough computational analysis reveals that the gradations resulting from these respective brackets are characterized by

\displaystyle\{\bar{D}_{2},\bar{I}_{1}\}=

\displaystyle\,(0,4,8)\tilde{+}(1,3,8)\tilde{+}(2,4,6)\tilde{+}(1,5,6);

\displaystyle\{\bar{D}_{3},\bar{I}_{1}\}=

\displaystyle\,(0,4,8)\tilde{+}(1,5,6)\tilde{+}(0,6,6);

\displaystyle\{\bar{D}_{4},\bar{I}_{1}\}=

\displaystyle\,(0,2,10)\tilde{+}(1,3,8)\tilde{+}(0,4,8);

\displaystyle\{\bar{D}_{2},\bar{I}_{2}\}=

\displaystyle\,(3,1,8)\tilde{+}(4,0,8)\tilde{+}(5,1,6)\tilde{+}(4,2,6);

(4.66)

\displaystyle\{\bar{D}_{3},\bar{I}_{2}\}=

\displaystyle\,(2,2,8)\tilde{+}(3,1,8)\tilde{+}(4,2,6)\tilde{+}(3,3,6);

\displaystyle\{\bar{D}_{4},\bar{I}_{2}\}=

\displaystyle\,(2,0,10)\tilde{+}(4,0,8)\tilde{+}(3,1,8).

As an example, we will provide the allowed terms from the homogeneous grading of
\{\bar{D}_{2},\bar{I}_{2}\}
. A direct calculation shows that

\displaystyle(3,1,8)=

\displaystyle\,\left\{\bar{b}_{3}\bar{D}_{2}\bar{F}_{2},\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{3}\bar{D}_{2}\bar{D}_{4},\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{3}^{3}\bar{D}_{2},C^{(003)}\bar{C}_{2}\bar{F}_{2},C^{(003)}C^{(202)}\bar{E}_{1},C^{(202)}\bar{D}_{2}\bar{D}_{4},\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{3}C^{(003)}\bar{E}_{1},\bar{b}_{1}\left(C^{(003)}\right)^{2}\bar{D}_{2},\bar{b}_{1}C^{(003)}\bar{C}_{2}\bar{D}_{4},\right.

\displaystyle\,\left.\bar{b}_{3}^{2}C^{(202)}\bar{D}_{2},\bar{b}_{3}C^{(003)}\bar{C}_{2}C^{(202)},\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{3}^{2}C^{(003)}\bar{C}_{2}\right\};

\displaystyle(4,0,8)=

\displaystyle\,\left\{\bar{F}_{2}^{2},\bar{b}_{1}\bar{D}_{4}\bar{F}_{2},\bar{b}_{1}^{2}\bar{b}_{3}^{2}\bar{D}_{4},\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{3}^{2}\bar{F}_{2},\bar{b}_{1}^{2}\bar{b}_{3}^{4},\bar{b}_{1}^{2}\bar{D}_{4}^{2},\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{3}C^{(202)}\bar{D}_{4},\bar{b}_{1}\left(C^{(003)}\right)^{2}C^{(202)},\bar{b}_{3}^{2}\left(C^{(202)}\right)^{2},\bar{b}_{3}C^{(202)}\bar{F}_{2},\right.

\displaystyle\,\left.\left(C^{(202)}\right)^{2}\bar{D}_{4},\bar{b}_{1}^{2}\bar{b}_{3}\left(C^{(003)}\right)^{2},\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{3}^{3}C^{(202)}\right\};

\displaystyle(5,1,6)=

\displaystyle\,\left\{\bar{b}_{1}\bar{D}_{2}\bar{F}_{2},\left(C^{(202)}\right)^{2}\bar{D}_{2},\bar{b}_{1}^{2}C^{(003)}\bar{E}_{1},\bar{b}_{1}^{2}\bar{D}_{2}\bar{D}_{4},\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{3}C^{(202)}\bar{D}_{2},\bar{b}_{1}C^{(003)}\bar{C}_{2}C^{(202)},\bar{b}_{1}^{2}\bar{b}_{3}^{2}\bar{D}_{2},\bar{b}_{1}^{2}\bar{b}_{3}C^{(003)}\bar{C}_{2}\right\};

\displaystyle(4,2,6)=

\displaystyle\,\left\{\bar{F}_{1}^{2}\bar{b}_{1}\bar{D}_{3}\bar{F}_{2},\bar{b}_{1}\bar{E}_{1}^{2}\bar{C}_{2}^{2}\bar{F}_{2},\bar{b}_{1}^{2}\bar{b}_{3}\bar{F}_{4},\bar{b}_{1}^{2}\bar{D}_{3}\bar{D}_{4},\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{2}\bar{b}_{3}\bar{F}_{2},\bar{b}_{1}^{2}\bar{b}_{2}\bar{b}_{3}\bar{D}_{4},\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{3}\bar{C}_{2}\bar{E}_{1},\bar{b}_{1}\bar{C}_{2}^{2}\bar{D}_{4},\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{3}\bar{D}_{2}^{2},\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{3}^{2}\bar{C}_{2}^{2},\bar{b}_{1}^{2}\bar{b}_{2}\bar{b}_{3}^{3},\bar{b}_{1}^{2}\bar{b}_{3}^{2}\bar{D}_{3},\right.

\displaystyle\,\left.\bar{b}_{2}C^{(202)}\bar{F}_{2},,\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{2}C^{(202)}\bar{D}_{4},\bar{b}_{1}C^{(003)}\bar{C}_{2}\bar{D}_{2},\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{3}C^{(202)}\bar{D}_{3},\bar{b}_{2}\bar{b}_{3}\left(C^{(202)}\right)^{2},\bar{b}_{3}\bar{C}_{2}^{2}C^{(202)},\bar{b}_{1}^{2}\bar{b}_{2}\left(C^{(003)}\right)^{2},\bar{b}_{1}\bar{b}_{2}\bar{b}_{3}^{2}C^{(202)},\right.

\displaystyle\,\left.\bar{b}_{1}C^{(202)}\bar{F}_{4},\bar{C}_{2}C^{(202)}\bar{E}_{1},\left(C^{(202)}\right)^{2}\bar{D}_{3},C^{(202)}\bar{D}_{3}^{2}\right\}.

After the usual change of the generators, we have that the bracket
\{\bar{D}_{2},\bar{I}_{2}\}
contains
149
admissible terms, which means there are coefficients
\Gamma_{49}^{1},\ldots,\Gamma_{49}^{149}
such that

\displaystyle\{\bar{D}_{2},\bar{I}_{2}\}=\Gamma_{49}^{1}\bar{b}_{3}\bar{D}_{2}\bar{F}_{2}+\ldots+\Gamma_{49}^{148}\bar{D}_{2}^{2}\bar{D}_{3}+\Gamma_{49}^{149}\bar{D}_{2}\bar{D}_{3}^{2}.

Determining these coefficients will lead to the explicit expansion of this brackets, which is given by

\displaystyle\{\bar{D}_{2},\bar{I}_{2}\}

\displaystyle=\frac{{\rm i}}{32}\bigl(4(\bar{d}_{1}+\bar{D}_{2}-\bar{D}_{3}+2\bar{D}_{4})\bigl(2\bar{d}_{1}\bar{D}_{2}+5\bar{D}_{2}^{2}-2(4\bar{d}_{1}+5\bar{D}_{2})\bar{D}_{3}+8\bar{D}_{3}^{2}+4(3\bar{D}_{2}-4\bar{D}_{3})\bar{D}_{4}

\displaystyle\qquad-16\bar{b}_{3}\bar{C}_{2}^{2}+12\bar{c}_{1}\bar{E}_{1}\bigl)-192\bar{b}_{2}^{2}\bar{b}_{3}(\bar{b}_{3}(\bar{d}_{1}+\bar{D}_{2}-\bar{D}_{3}+2\bar{D}_{4})-\bar{F}_{2})-48(\bar{c}_{1}-2\bar{C}_{2})\bar{C}_{2}\bar{F}_{2}

\displaystyle\qquad-32\bar{b}_{3}\bar{D}_{2}\bar{F}_{2}-48\bar{b}_{2}\bigl((\bar{d}_{1}+\bar{D}_{2}-\bar{D}_{3}+2\bar{D}_{4})\bar{F}_{2}-\bar{b}_{3}\bigl(4\bar{b}_{3}\bar{C}_{2}^{2}+\bar{d}_{1}^{2}+2\bar{d}_{1}(\bar{D}_{2}+\bar{D}_{3})+2\bar{D}_{2}\bar{D}_{3}

\displaystyle\qquad-3\bar{D}_{3}^{2}+4(\bar{d}_{1}+\bar{D}_{2}+\bar{D}_{3})\bar{D}_{4}+4\bar{D}_{4}^{2}-8\bar{C}_{2}\bar{E}_{1}\bigl)\bigl)+4\bar{b}_{1}\bigl(18\bar{b}_{3}^{2}\bar{C}_{2}^{2}+24\bar{b}_{2}^{2}(\bar{b}_{3}^{3}-\bar{b}_{3}\bar{D}_{4})

\displaystyle\qquad+(\bar{c}_{1}-\bar{C}_{2})\bar{C}_{2}(\bar{d}_{1}+5\bar{D}_{2}-\bar{D}_{3})+2(3\bar{c}_{1}-8\bar{C}_{2})\bar{C}_{2}\bar{D}_{4}-4\bar{E}_{1}^{2}+2\bar{b}_{3}\bigl(\bar{d}_{1}(\bar{D}_{2}+4\bar{D}_{3})+3\bar{D}_{2}\bar{D}_{3}

\displaystyle\qquad-4\bar{D}_{3}^{2}+2(\bar{D}_{2}+4\bar{D}_{3})\bar{D}_{4}-2(\bar{c}_{1}+4\bar{C}_{2})\bar{E}_{1}\bigl)-4(\bar{D}_{2}-2\bar{D}_{3})\bar{F}_{2}+2\bar{b}_{2}\bigl(4\bar{b}_{3}(\bar{F}_{2}+6\bar{F}_{4})

(4.67)

\displaystyle\qquad+3\bar{D}_{4}(\bar{d}_{1}+\bar{D}_{2}-\bar{D}_{3}+2\bar{D}_{4})-\bar{b}_{3}^{2}(7(\bar{d}_{1}+\bar{D}_{2})+17\bar{D}_{3}+14\bar{D}_{4})\bigl)\bigl)\bigl)\,.

Repeating the same argument, the rest of the degree-twelve expansions in the compact form
\{\bar{\textbf{D}},\bar{\textbf{I}}\}
close as follows:

\displaystyle\{\bar{D}_{2},\bar{I}_{1}\}

\displaystyle=\frac{{\rm i}}{32}\bigl(\bar{b}_{1}\bigl(\bar{b}_{2}^{2}(\bar{c}_{1}-\bar{C}_{2})^{2}+24\bar{b}_{2}\bar{D}_{3}(\bar{D}_{4}-\bar{b}_{3}^{2})+48\bar{D}_{3}(\bar{b}_{3}\bar{D}_{3}-\bar{F}_{4})\bigl)+4\bar{b}_{2}^{2}(\bar{c}_{1}-\bar{C}_{2})(\bar{b}_{3}\bar{C}_{2}-2\bar{E}_{1})

\displaystyle\qquad+4\bar{D}_{3}\bigl(3\bar{D}_{2}^{2}-16\bar{b}_{3}\bar{C}_{2}^{2}-8\bar{D}_{3}(\bar{d}_{1}-\bar{D}_{3}+2\bar{D}_{4})+\bar{D}_{2}(8\bar{D}_{4}-6\bar{D}_{3})+12\bar{c}_{1}\bar{E}_{1}\bigl)-16\bar{F}_{4}(2\bar{b}_{3}\bar{D}_{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+3(\bar{c}_{1}-2\bar{C}_{2})\bar{C}_{2})+4\bar{b}_{2}\bigl((6\bar{b}_{3}^{2}-2\bar{D}_{2}-\bar{D}_{3}-10\bar{D}_{4})\bar{C}_{2}^{2}+(2\bar{D}_{2}+\bar{D}_{3}+4\bar{D}_{4})\bar{c}_{1}\bar{C}_{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+2\bar{b}_{3}\bar{D}_{3}(4\bar{d}_{1}+5\bar{D}_{2}-4\bar{D}_{3}+8\bar{D}_{4})-4\bar{b}_{3}\bar{c}_{1}\bar{E}_{1}+4\bar{E}_{1}^{2}-8\bar{D}_{3}\bar{F}_{2}-4\bar{D}_{2}\bar{F}_{4}\bigl)\bigl)\,,

\displaystyle\{\bar{D}_{3},\bar{I}_{1}\}

\displaystyle=\frac{{\rm i}}{8}\bigl(2\bar{D}_{3}^{2}(12\bar{b}_{3}^{2}+\bar{D}_{2}-4\bar{D}_{4})-\bar{b}_{2}\bar{D}_{3}\bigl(8\bar{b}_{3}^{3}+3\bar{c}_{1}^{2}-7\bar{c}_{1}\bar{C}_{2}+4\bar{C}_{2}^{2}-2\bar{b}_{3}(\bar{D}_{2}+4\bar{D}_{4})\bigl)-64\bar{b}_{3}\bar{D}_{3}\bar{F}_{4}

\displaystyle\qquad+\bar{b}_{2}^{2}(\bar{c}_{1}-\bar{C}_{2})(\bar{b}_{3}\bar{c}_{1}-\bar{E}_{1})+4\bar{b}_{2}(4\bar{b}_{3}^{2}-\bar{D}_{2}-4\bar{D}_{4})\bar{F}_{4}+48\bar{F}_{4}^{2}\bigl)\,,

\displaystyle\{\bar{D}_{4},\bar{I}_{1}\}

\displaystyle=\frac{{\rm i}}{32}\bigl(\bar{D}_{3}\bigl(12(\bar{d}_{1}+\bar{D}_{2}-\bar{D}_{3})\bar{D}_{3}+48\bar{b}_{3}^{2}\bar{D}_{3}+12\bar{b}_{3}(\bar{C}_{2}^{2}-\bar{b}_{1}\bar{D}_{3})+8(\bar{D}_{3}-2\bar{D}_{2})\bar{D}_{4}

\displaystyle\qquad-3(\bar{D}_{2}^{2}+8\bar{c}_{1}\bar{E}_{1})\bigl)+2\bar{b}_{2}^{2}\bar{b}_{3}(\bar{c}_{1}-\bar{C}_{2})^{2}+4\bar{F}_{4}\bigl(6(\bar{c}_{1}-\bar{C}_{2})\bar{C}_{2}+4\bar{b}_{3}(\bar{D}_{2}-8\bar{D}_{3})+3\bar{b}_{1}\bar{D}_{3}\bigl)

\displaystyle\qquad+96\bar{F}_{4}^{2}-2\bar{b}_{2}\bigl((8\bar{b}_{3}^{3}+3\bar{c}_{1}^{2}-6\bar{c}_{1}\bar{C}_{2}+3\bar{C}_{2}^{2})\bar{D}_{3}+4(\bar{c}_{1}\bar{C}_{2}-\bar{C}_{2}^{2})\bar{D}_{4}+3\bar{b}_{1}\bar{D}_{3}(\bar{D}_{4}-\bar{b}_{3}^{2})

4.10.
Expansions in the degree
15
brackets

The compact forms in degree fifteen brackets are given by
\{\bar{\textbf{G}},\bar{\textbf{I}}\}
and
\{\bar{\textbf{H}},\bar{\textbf{H}}\}

Beginning with
\{\bar{\textbf{G}},\bar{\textbf{I}}\}
, a direct computation shows that the gradings are

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}\left(\{\bar{G}_{1},\bar{I}_{1}\}\right)=

\displaystyle\,(1,4,10)\tilde{+}(2,3,10)\tilde{+}(3,4,8)\tilde{+}(2,5,8);

(4.98)

\displaystyle\qquad-2\bar{D}_{3}\bar{H}_{1}+6\bar{D}_{3}\bar{H}_{2}\bigl)+\bar{c}_{1}\bigl(-\bar{C}_{2}^{2}\bar{F}_{3}+14\bar{F}_{1}\bar{F}_{4}-6\bar{F}_{3}\bar{F}_{4}+18\bar{E}_{1}\bar{G}_{2}+20\bar{D}_{3}\bar{H}_{2}\bigl)\bigl)\bigl)+16\bar{b}_{2}^{2}\bar{b}_{3}^{2}\bar{I}_{2}+96\bar{D}_{3}^{2}\bar{I}_{2}

\displaystyle\qquad-2\bar{b}_{2}\bigl(4\bigl(-2\bar{E}_{1}\bar{F}_{1}+\bar{D}_{2}\bar{G}_{1}+7\bigl(\bar{d}_{1}+\bar{D}_{2}-\bar{D}_{3}+2\bar{D}_{4}\bigl)\bar{G}_{2}\bigl)\bar{b}_{3}^{2}+\bigl(\bar{C}_{2}\bigl(17\bar{D}_{3}-4\bar{d}_{1}\bigl)\bar{F}_{1}+24\bar{C}_{2}\bigl(\bar{d}_{1}+\bar{D}_{2}

\displaystyle\qquad-\bar{D}_{3}+2\bar{D}_{4}\bigl)\bar{F}_{3}+16\bigl(\bar{F}_{2}\bar{G}_{2}-7\bar{E}_{1}\bar{H}_{1}\bigl)+28\bigl(\bar{D}_{2}+2\bar{D}_{3}\bigl)\bar{I}_{2}\bigl)\bar{b}_{3}+3\bar{c}_{1}^{2}\bar{C}_{2}\bar{F}_{1}-\bar{c}_{1}\bigl(3\bar{F}_{1}\bar{C}_{2}^{2}-8\bar{b}_{3}\bar{C}_{2}\bar{G}_{1}

\displaystyle\qquad+13\bar{b}_{3}\bigl(2\bigl(\bar{d}_{1}+\bar{D}_{2}+2\bar{D}_{4}\bigl)\bar{F}_{3}+\bar{D}_{3}\bigl(\bar{F}_{1}-2\bar{F}_{3}\bigl)\bigl)+2\bar{F}_{2}\bigl(5\bar{F}_{1}-9\bar{F}_{3}\bigl)-42\bar{E}_{1}\bar{G}_{1}\bigl)-8\bar{D}_{4}^{2}\bar{G}_{2}+4\bar{D}_{4}\bigl(18\bar{E}_{1}\bar{F}_{1}

(4.108)

\displaystyle\qquad-\bigl(\bar{d}_{1}+\bar{D}_{2}-\bar{D}_{3}\bigl)\bar{G}_{2}+6\bar{C}_{2}\bar{H}_{2}\bigl)+2\bar{C}_{2}\bigl(7\bar{F}_{1}\bar{F}_{2}-12\bar{F}_{3}\bar{F}_{2}-27\bar{E}_{1}\bar{G}_{1}+6\bigl(\bar{d}_{1}+\bar{D}_{2}-\bar{D}_{3}\bigl)\bar{H}_{2}\bigl)\bigl)\bigl)\,.

These relations show that the
20
generators in
\mathcal{P}
form a polynomial algebra of degree
4
, i.e.,
\textbf{Alg}\left\langle\mathcal{P}\right\rangle:=\mathcal{Q}_{\mathfrak{su}(4)}(4)
is closed under the Poisson bracket
\{\cdot,\cdot\}.
It merely remains to determine the additional dependence relations satisfied by the generators.
